# JumpingBallsLive – satisfying ball physics simulator

JumpingBallsLive is a full-featured, editable, free, browser-based
bouncing-ball physics simulator that exports vertical MP4 clips for TikTok, Reels and Shorts.

Built with **Next.js 15 (App Router) · React 19 · TypeScript · Tailwind CSS v4 · next-intl**.
Everything runs client-side: physics, rendering, audio and video encoding happen in the visitor's browser.
The site is exported as plain static files, so it deploys to **GitHub Pages** (workflow included) or any
static host – no server required.

## Features

Every range given below is a slider's **comfort range** only: the number field next to every slider takes any value past it – huge, tiny or decimal – and nothing is capped (see [Uncapped](#uncapped)).

| Area | What you get |
| --- | --- |
| **Game modes** | One per id in `MODE_IDS` (`src/lib/physics/types.ts`; the pages and metadata fill `{count}` with `MODE_CARD_ORDER.length`, every mode having a card) – each a small plugin class, grouped into families on the mode cards: **escape** (the ring modes – Classic, Accumulation, Multiply, Lines, Paint, Target, Portal, Shatter, Color Match, Grow – plus Power Layers and Multipliers), **rhythm** (the sound-first modes: Ball Drop, Bouncing Shapes, Pendulum Wave, Metronomes & Polyrhythms, Collision Playground, Circle Illusion, Square Battle Royale, Capture the Flag, Beat Runner, Paddle Keep-Up, Beat Drop, Double Pendulum Harp, Square Racing Grand Prix, Bullseye, Sound Vortex, Glass Smash), **battle** (String Battle, Territory, Maze Escape) and **journey** (Journey) |
| **Physics** | Ball speed, size, gravity, bounciness ("bouncier each hit"), two balls, wall count, thickness, gap size, rotation |
| **Physics extras** | Air drag, horizontal and vertical wind, spin (wall contact spins the ball, a Magnus-style force curves its flight, custom ball images and emoji rotate with it), wall bounciness (restitution), breathing walls (radii pulse, gaps follow; collision-safe at every amplitude and speed) and rotating gravity – all deterministic (seeds and Find Simulation include them) and off by default, in the "Advanced physics" groups of the Ball and Wall sections, shared via the URL (`drag`, `wx`, `wy`, `spin`, `wb`, `bw`, `bws`, `rg`) |
| **Merge & split balls** | A ball-interaction setting in the Ball section: balls **bounce** (default), **merge** into one bigger ball on contact (area and momentum conserved, colours blended, a particle burst and a low tone), **split** in two every time a ball breaks through a wall (half the area each, diverging velocities, a high tone – down to a smallest size and up to a ball cap) or **pass** through each other. Works in the two-ball modes and Multiply, is deterministic (seeds and Find Simulation include it) and shared via the URL (`bi`, `smr`, `mb`) |
| **Ball Drop & obstacles** | A ring-less "symphony" mode: a tall board of pegs and short bars in staggered rows, closed by left, right and bottom walls, fills the canvas height; 1–40 balls of different sizes and weights are released from the top and every peg, bar or wall hit plays a note pitched by the ball's size (bigger = lower, snapped to the current scale), so the mix of gravities and sizes turns the run into a polyrhythm until every ball has come to rest (a board too small for its balls – many big ones – finishes once it is full, with the balls it could hold) – or forever with **Rain** on (the floor opens and balls re-enter at the top). Settings in the Mode row: ball count, size spread, gravity spread, peg rows, release interval, rain (`dbc`, `dsv`, `dgv`, `drows`, `dsi`, `dloop`); deterministic, so seeds and Find Simulation include it. Built on a generic obstacle layer (`src/lib/physics/obstacles.ts`: circles and optionally spinning bars with restitution, friction and swept collision maths) that any mode – or an obstacle editor – can reuse; the canvas draws obstacles in the wall colour with the wall glow |
| **Bouncing Shapes & DVD polyrhythms** | A rectangular box (portrait by default, `boxAspect`) instead of rings, with 1–12 axis-aligned squares, circles or rounded DVD-style logos (`boxShape`) flying in straight lines at constant speed with perfect reflections (mirror reflection, so the bounce period never drifts; gravity optional via `boxGravity`). Every wall hit plays the next note of the loaded melody or, without one, one of four notes – one per wall (C5 E5 G5 C6, snapped to the scale, on the beat grid). A big number on each shape counts down by one per hit (`boxCountdown`, 0–99; the run ends when every shape reached zero, never at 0), the shape flashes, grows a little (`boxGrowPerHit`, 0–3 %) and cycles its colour. Shapes at speeds in small whole-number ratios (`boxSpeedRatio` 1:1 / 2:3 / 3:4:5 / 4:5:6) hit the walls in those ratios – the polyrhythm – and a DVD logo that reaches a corner plays an accent (louder, longer note plus a full-screen flash). Deterministic: the seed picks the axis ratios, the launch directions and the run's tempo (±15 % on every speed, the ratios intact), so run lengths vary continuously and Find Simulation works while the countdown is on (with it off the finder says the run never ends instead of searching); a DVD logo always gets an odd axis ratio and a corner lock re-aims it by a hair at every bounce, so it reaches a corner exactly every 3–13 hits even while it grows. In the "Box arena" block of the Mode row, shared via the URL (`bxn`, `bxs`, `bxa`, `bxg`, `bxc`, `bxgr`, `bxr`) |
| **Pendulum Wave** | The project.jdm pendulum-wave / phasing format: 5–60 pendulums tuned like the classic Harvard wave (pendulum *i* completes K + *i* swings per cycle of T seconds, default 51 + *i* in 60 s), so the row starts in line, drifts into travelling waves, splits into two and three groups and snaps back in line at the end of every cycle – analytic motion θᵢ(t) = A cos(2π fᵢ t) from the simulation clock, so it is exactly deterministic and replays frame for frame. Six layouts: **row** (pivots on a bar, strings ∝ 1/f²), **arc** (pivots on a ring, swinging toward the centre – "Pendulum Wave in a Circle"), **circle** (bobs on radii from the centre, optionally mapped onto a rotating **polygon** of 3–8 sides), **galaxy** (the radial layout on a slowly turning frame – spiral arms in the trails), **sliding** (bobs on rails) and **bouncing** (balls on a floor with a rectified free-fall arc, so every ball hits the floor at its own frequency and the slow ones bounce highest); **phasing** swaps the harmonic series for an arithmetic series of swing times (a Reich-style tempo phase shift that never quite realigns); fading **trails** 0–1; rainbow colours by index. Sound: a note when a bob crosses the centre, reaches an extreme or both (the floor hit / apex when bouncing), pitched by the pendulum's index as a major-scale degree from C4 (low → high or high → low), through the ToneGenerator like every other hit (instrument, scale snap, beat lock, hit sample, melody); the **wave chord** option plays bobs whose events fall within 20 ms as one chord (`SoundEvent.chord`: one grid slot, the level shared out, a flash when most of the row aligns). Finishes after N full cycles (default 1) or never; the finder knows the run length is fixed by the settings and says so. In the "Pendulum wave" block of the Mode row, shared via the URL (`pwn`, `pwk`, `pwt`, `pwa`, `pwl`, `pwp`, `pwph`, `pwtr`, `pws`, `pwpd`, `pwch`, `pwc`) |
| **Visuals** | Rainbow walls (gradient / pulse), ball & wall glow, colour trail, trail thickness, reactive background, camera follow, wall-break effects (confetti, shatter, shockwave, all, none), custom ball image or emoji, top/bottom text overlays, watermark |
| **Drama director** | A hidden "cinematic" layer that nudges rebounds for near-misses and dramatic escapes (toggle in advanced options) |
| **Sound** | Synthesised bounce tones, 12 built-in public-domain melodies (MIDI), custom MIDI import, custom hit samples (3 built-in clips or your own upload on every bounce, optionally pitched per wall, included in recordings), song slicer (upload any MP3/OGG/WAV/M4A and every bounce plays the next slice of it, with a song progress bar in the HUD and in the recording), custom wall-break sound clips |
| **Instruments, scales & BPM lock** | Seven bounce voices (sine, triangle, square, saw, Karplus-Strong pluck, FM marimba, chip blip) for the wall tones and, separately, for melody notes; snap every wall tone and melody note to a scale (major, minor, pentatonic, blues, whole tone) on any root note; and a beat lock that schedules sounds (voices and hit samples alike) on a 60–200 BPM grid (1/4, 1/8, 1/16) so exports sit on the beat – all shareable via the URL (`inst`, `minst`, `scale`, `root`, `qz`, `bpm`, `grid`) |
| **Background music with ducking** | Upload any audio file (kept in memory) as a music bed under the bounce sounds: volume, sidechain ducking that dips the bed on every bounce and wall break and swells it back over an adjustable release, loop, start offset; the bed starts, pauses, restarts and stops with the run, shows a live level meter in the panel and is mixed into the exported video, so clips are post-ready (`mv`, `md`, `mdr`, `mloop`, `mso`) |
| **Picture Paint** | Paint mode can reveal any picture: upload a PNG, JPG, WebP or GIF (kept in memory), it is cover-fitted into the arena and a dim greyscale ghost shows what is coming while the ball's brush uncovers it stroke by stroke through a full-resolution mask; **beat sync** detects the tempo of the loaded song (music bed or song slicer – spectral-flux onsets and autocorrelation, computed in idle slices) or takes the manual BPM, and makes the ball kick on every beat and glide in between, the brush and the glow pulsing with it; **guided coverage** steers every rebound (≤ 18°) toward the largest unrevealed area; **pace to song** re-sizes the brush every second so the reveal finishes when the song or the clip ends, with an on-schedule / behind / ahead hint in the HUD. Deterministic (recordings replay exactly), in the Mode row of Paint mode, shared via the URL (`pbr`, `pgh`, `pbeat`, `pbs`, `pbp`, `pgd`, `pps`); without a picture Paint mode is exactly the classic trail |
| **Recording** | MediaRecorder export in 500×500, 1280×720, 1920×1080 or 1080×1920, 10–120 s, with audio; MP4 only with H.264 + AAC (Safari, Chrome builds with an H.264 encoder), WebM (VP9 / VP8 + Opus) elsewhere – never VP9 inside an .mp4. Record Video on a finished run records that run again from its seed (what the fast export renders), not the frozen end screen |
| **Find Simulation** | Deterministic, seeded physics lets the finder search for a seed whose run lasts exactly N seconds – 1,000 seeds, and when none of them matched and seeds are cheap it searches deeper, for up to 25 s in all |
| **Presets & sharing** | Save/load presets in localStorage; every setting is mirrored into the URL for bookmarking and sharing |
| **Controls UX** | Collapsible sections, setting search, advanced-options toggle, per-section reset, keyboard shortcuts (Space, R), 1×–8× playback speed, FPS counter, auto-pause when off-screen |
| **Site** | Landing page (hero, mode cards, about, how-it-works, illustrated instructions, features, FAQ), simulator page with editorial sections and troubleshooting, About, TikTok page, Feedback form (GitHub issue / email / any form endpoint), Privacy, Terms, Disclaimer, localised 404 |
| **i18n & SEO** | English, Polish and Spanish (`/en`, `/pl`, `/es`), hreflang alternates, sitemap, robots, Open Graph, JSON-LD (WebSite, WebApplication, FAQPage) |
| **Hosting** | Static export (`out/`) with base-path support, GitHub Actions workflow that lints, tests, builds and publishes to GitHub Pages, localised 404 page, root page that redirects to the visitor's language |
| **Ball characters & the Gerald persona** | A vector face on the ball in every mode – **Face** none / dot / cute / cool / cat / angry (URL `face`): two eyes whose pupils look along the flight, a blink on a seeded schedule (the same seed blinks at the same moments in every run and every recording) and expressions driven by what happens – an "ouch" squint on hard hits, wide eyes when a wall breaks, a grin at the escape or the finish, closed happy eyes at rest; the cat adds ears and whiskers and, with **Cat Sounds** (`fsnd`) while no hit sample is used, a synthesised meow-like chirp through the ToneGenerator (beat lock, scale snap, music-bed ducking, recording). A **name label** under the ball with a subtle outline (`bn`, `nl`), render-only **squash & stretch** on impact (`sq`, 0–1), the face over a custom image or emoji as an option (`fimg`) and a **Meet Gerald** button (the site's demo persona: cute face, name "Gerald", squash). Drawn procedurally into the canvas (so recordings have it) and scaled with the ball size, on the Bouncing Shapes, Pendulum Wave, Metronomes & Polyrhythms and Collision Playground bodies too (the first 80 of a crowd); purely visual, so seeds and Find Simulation are untouched. In the "Character" group at the top of the Ball section, with a live preview of the face |
| **Themes & backgrounds** | One-click looks at the top of the Visual section: ten curated themes (Neon, Pastel, Mono, Sunset, Ocean, Retro, Candy, Matrix, Aurora, Luxe) as small colour-swatch cards. Picking one merges its wall, ball, line, background and trail colours and its particle style into the settings – every colour stays individually editable, the card then reads "edited" and a second click restores the theme; the Default card goes back to the plain look. Backgrounds: solid, top-to-bottom gradient or an uploaded picture (kept in memory, cover-fitted, darkened by a dim slider); the reactive background still flashes on top, and vertical exports continue the background into the letterbox bars. Particle styles for the bursts at wall breaks and finishes: confetti (default), sparks, petals, pixels, bubbles – visual only, so seeds and Find Simulation are unaffected. Colour trail in two custom colours instead of the rainbow. Shared via the URL (`theme`, `bgt`, `bg1`, `bg2`, `bgd`, `ps`, `trc`; the picture never travels) |
| **Metronomes & Polyrhythms** | A rhythm-family mode (`polyrhythm`) for the project.jdm polyrhythm / tempo-phase-shift formats: 2–400 voices tick at their own tempos and every tick is a note. Four layouts – **rings** (a dot per concentric ring orbiting a whole number of times per cycle, ticking at 12 o'clock – the 3:4:5 visualiser; **polygons** draws every ring as a polygon with as many corners as its ratio, turning once per cycle), **arcs** (dots sliding along the parallel chords of a circle or along concentric semicircles, ticking at both ends), **metronomes** (a grid of pendulum metronomes ticking at both extremes) and **spiral** (orbiting dots whose tick points lie on a spiral) – switchable mid-run. Tempos: **harmonic** (ratios 1…N per cycle), **BPM steps** (base BPM + i × step, a Reich-style tempo phase shift) or a **custom** ratio list such as `3,4,5,7`. The moment every voice ticks together is computed exactly and plays an accented chord with a screen flash; accent every k-th tick, pitch by voice (scale degree) or by ratio (harmonics of C3), optional numbers on the dots; finishes after N cycles (always the same length – the finder says so) or never. Exact integer arithmetic on the step counter, so 400 voices replay bit for bit and stay cheap. Settings in the Mode row, shared via the URL (`prn`, `prl`, `pras`, `prt`, `prcu`, `prcs`, `prb`, `prbs`, `prp`, `pra`, `prpb`, `prnum`, `prc`) |
| **Collision Playground** | A rhythm-family mode without rings (id `collide`, the project.jdm "1247 varied bouncing orbs" formats): 10–2000 orbs of varied sizes (masses ∝ area) bounce around a circle or a square and off each other with elastic collisions (restitution 0.7–1), with optional gravity. Collisions are solved with a uniform spatial hash grid rebuilt every sub-step (pairs only within neighbouring cells, position correction so piles do not sink), so 300 orbs cost a fraction of a millisecond per step and 2000 still run in real time. Every collision plays a soft note pitched by the smaller orb (bigger = lower, on a pentatonic ladder the ToneGenerator snaps to the scale; instruments, hit samples, beat lock and the music bed apply), at most 12 per rendered frame at any playback speed – the most energetic. Variants: **squishy** (squash-and-stretch along the contact normal, 120 ms), **sync start** (all orbs released from a grid at once – "Squishy BALLS bounce in SYNC"), **anti-collision** at a chosen second (collisions switch off, the orbs change colour and pass through each other) and **lollipops on a ring** (up to 36 bodies on a circular track with 1-D collisions, drawn as sticks from the centre to a disc). Deterministic (spawn from the seed); endless, so the finder says so. In the "Collision playground" block of the Mode row, shared via the URL (`cpn`, `cpsz`, `cpc`, `cpg`, `cpe`, `cpsq`, `cpsy`, `cpac`, `cpr`) |
| **Team balls & scoreboard** | The "which one wins" format. A **Ball Count** of 1–6 (it replaced the two-ball switch; old `two=1` links still mean two balls) in Classic, Multiply (the start count), Lines, Shatter and Color Match – and 1–2 in Grow, whose balls grow to fill the ring – the balls flying out in evenly spread directions, and a **team roster** in its own "Teams & Scoreboard" section of the panel: a name, a colour and an emoji per team (an emoji field with suggestions), one team per ball, added and removed with the ball count. Team balls wear their colour and emoji (a custom ball picture still wins; Color Match keeps its colour to match and rings each ball in its team colour), carry their team name above them (**Ball Names**) and hand the team down when they multiply or split. A **scoreboard** in the top-left or top-right corner of the exported square counts bounces, walls broken and escapes per team – the engine keeps per-ball stats – with the leader on top; when the run ends a **winner banner** (most escapes, then walls, then bounces) pops up with confetti and is held for 3 s, so recordings include it (an export whose length runs out right after the run waits for it, and Find Simulation adds the 3 s to the export length). The stats never touch the physics, so seeds and Find Simulation are unaffected; shared via the URL (`nb`, `teams=Red*ef4444*🔥,…`, `tn`, `tsb`, `tsp`) and saved in presets |
| **Cinematic camera** | A "Camera" group in the Visual section: **camera zoom** (0–1, `cz`) zooms the view up to 2.2× toward the first ball and follows it (0 keeps the classic view and Camera Follow; with several balls – the Ball Count – it zooms only as far as keeps them all in frame), **screen shake** (0–1, `shake`) on every wall break, dying out over 300 ms, **slow motion on near misses** (`slow`; speed 0.2–0.8× `slowf`, length 200–1500 ms `slowms`) – the Drama Director's near-miss test, exposed as an engine event, slows the clock that feeds the fixed-step engine, and the balls and walls are drawn between its steps so the slow motion glides instead of stuttering – and **replay on escape** (`replay`): when a ball escapes the outer wall (Classic, Accumulation, Portal, Shatter, Color Match) its last 2 s replay at half speed with a REPLAY badge before the end screen (team balls keep their colours, emoji and names, and the teams' winner banner waits for the replay). Rendering and time scale only – seeds, rigged outcomes and Find Simulation are untouched – and recordings capture all of it in real time (the export waits for the replay; a clip is measured on the run's pace – Record Video and Fast export extend it by the real time the slow motion added, `CinematicCamera.getSlowLagMs()`, at most the whole clip at the slowest factor, and the captions' countdown runs on the same clock) |
| **Glass Smash** | A Gerald-family mode (id `glass`, in the sound-first family of the mode cards) after the geraldbounces clip "Gerald is determined to smash all of the glass": a portrait shaft of 1–10 stages of horizontal glass panes – 3–30 in the first stage, a third more in every stage after – stands between the ball and a doorway marked HOME. The ball falls under the mode's own gravity; every landing cracks the pane under it (a procedural crack grows from the impact point, drawn from the seed), plays the next melody note or a C-major scale degree by row and re-launches the ball to a steady hop, so the notes come at an even, quickening pulse; the last of a pane's 1–5 hit points (one more every other stage, drawn thicker) shatters it into 8–20 shards with an accented note and the wall-break sound – a new glass clip (`public/wallBreak/glass.wav`) unless another one is chosen – and the ball crashes through. After the last pane of a stage the camera scrolls down to the next one under a "STAGE n" banner; from the second stage on some panes have holes the ball must miss, from the third some slide left and right; the final stage ends with the ball rolling into the doorway, a chord, confetti and "HOME!". Deterministic (seeded tempo, kicks, holes, slides, cracks and shards), so Find Simulation searches it (the default run lasts roughly 25–33 s). In the "Glass" block of the Mode row, shared via the URL (`glr`, `glhp`, `gls`, `glm`, `glh`) |
| **Multipliers** | Stackable, uncapped stat boosts for every mode (`src/lib/physics/multipliers.ts`): a ball carries **speed, size, damage, bounce and gravity** multipliers that stack multiplicatively with **no cap** by default (`mpu`; with it off `mpc` caps every stat). Speed scales the velocity at once and the cruising speed after it, size the radius (a ball too big for its ring bursts it, and a ball bigger than the arena **outgrows** it: the run ends with an "OUTGREW THE ARENA" banner and confetti), damage takes more hit points off Shatter segments, Target's countdown and the board's blockers and, from ×4 (`wst`), **smashes the rings** on contact without the gap, bounce scales the restitution (no cap: the rebound grows with it), gravity the ball's weight. **Pickups** in the ten ring modes (`mpk`, `mpr`, `mpty`, `mpl`): orbs – x2 SPEED, x1.5 SIZE, x2 DMG, x2 BALLS, x1.25 BOUNCE, x2 GRAV – float in at seeded times and places, and touching one stacks it with a burst and a rising arpeggio. **HUD badges** ("SPEED x16 · SIZE x4 · DMG x8 · BALLS 512") in the top left pop and glow when they change and are part of recordings. The **Multipliers mode** (escape family): a tall board the camera scrolls down with the lowest ball, rows of gates – count x2/x3/x5 (clones, up to 50–2000 balls), speed, size, damage, ÷2 and release doors – with splitter pegs, bumpers, funnels and blockers with hit points; a live HOME counter and "N Gerald made it home" at the end; spatial-hash collisions and batched drawing for hundreds of balls; a **target count** rigs Find Simulation for a run within 5 % of it (`mprw`, `mpgm`, `mpsb`, `mpmb`, `mptg`). **Physics safety**: adaptive sub-steps keep every ball under half its radius (≤ 4 px) per sub-step, up to 64 per step, then the clock slows (SLOW-MO) instead of letting a ball tunnel – ×1000 speed stays inside its rings. Deterministic, off by default |
| **Obstacle editor** | An **Obstacles** section in the ten ring modes places **pegs** (static discs), **bumpers** (discs that multiply the ball's speed by the **Bumper Boost** – 1–2× on its slider, any boost typed – on every hard hit, with no speed cap, swell, flash white and play a pinball ding), **blockers** (static bars) and **spinners** (bars turning at −120…120 rpm on the slider, any spin typed) inside the arena: pick a kind in the dropdown and add it on a free spot, set each one's size (length, angle and spin for the bars) in the list – every row slider has a number field that takes any value (a position past the arena too), up to 1,000 obstacles –, remove one or clear them all. Before the start or while paused the obstacles are dragged on the canvas with the mouse, a pen or a finger (the ready screen shrinks to a bar so they stay visible) and Backspace / Delete removes the selected one; each row of the Obstacles list also has x / y sliders, so a layout can be built with the keyboard alone. Positions are in arena radii and sizes in percent of the arena radius, so a layout scales with the canvas; it is shared via the URL (`obs=p:0.2,-0.3,6;b:-0.4,0.1,8;k:x,y,length,angle;s:x,y,length,angle,rpm`, `obb` for the boost), saved in presets, kept across mode changes and deterministic (seeds and Find Simulation include it) |
| **Animated captions** | A "Captions" section of the panel with any number of overlays (up to the memory-safety ceiling of 1,000) beyond the top / bottom text, each with a type, a position (top, center or bottom of the exported square – captions at the same position stack), a start and an end time in seconds (end 0 = until the end of the run), an entrance / exit animation (**fade**, **slide** in from its edge, **pop** with a bounce) and a style (size, text colour, background pill or a soft shadow). **Countdown** shows the time left to the end of the clip (the recording length, `0:27`, pulsing on every tick of the last five seconds; an optional label with `[time]`), **wall counter** the walls broken over the walls in play (`Wall 3/10`, translated; `[n]` / `[total]` in a custom label; hidden in the modes without rings), **progress** a bar of the elapsed time over the clip length (optional label with `[pct]`), **question** a question ("Will it escape?") whose answer pops in underneath the moment a ball escapes or the run finishes (a finished run holds its end screen – and a recording – for 2 s so the answer is seen), and **text** any text (wrapped onto up to three lines). Drawn on the canvas inside the square the recorder crops, so every export has them, and timed by the simulation clock (a pause holds them, 8× speeds them up, Restart starts them over); render-only – seeds and the finder are untouched. Shared via the URL as one compact parameter (`cap=q*t*0*0*p*1.3*ffffff*000000*Will it escape?*YES!,…`), saved in presets, kept across mode changes |
| **Double Pendulum Harp & sparring** | A rhythm-family mode (`doublePendulum`) for the project.jdm "Double Pendulum HARP" and "2 Pendulums SPAR with Each Other" formats: 1–4 double pendulums – or triple pendulums – swing from the middle of the video, integrated in error-controlled Dormand–Prince 5(4) sub-steps (at least eight per 60 Hz step, hundreds where a light bob over a heavy one whips round; without friction the energy holds to within 1e-5 of Σm·g·L over two minutes for every rig the sliders allow), with arm lengths, bob masses, gravity, friction (0–0.01 of the speed per step) and start angles – or a seeded random start – to set; several pendulums start 0.05° apart and fly apart after a few seconds (the butterfly effect). **Harp**: 0–24 vertical or radial strings tuned to the scale and root of the Sound section (a major scale while it is chromatic) across 1–4 octaves; every bob that crosses a string plucks it – the crossing is solved on every sub-step, the string vibrates with a decaying sine and plays its note through the ToneGenerator (instrument, scale snap, beat lock, hit samples, melody, music-bed ducking), the plucks of one step as one chord. **Sparring**: two mirrored pendulums side by side whose bobs collide elastically (the impulse goes into both chains' angular velocities through the bobs), every hit a low, percussive note – accented when hard – and a flash. Long rainbow trails from the last bobs, faces on the bobs; the run ends with the clip (the finder says so) – its last 1.5 s a finale in which the rig holds still under a "TIME!" banner while a closing chord rings, so an export ends on it – or never. In the "Double pendulum" block of the Mode row, shared via the URL (`dpn`, `dpsg`, `dpl1`–`dpl3`, `dpm1`–`dpm3`, `dpg`, `dpa1`–`dpa3`, `dprs`, `dpd`, `dptr`, `dpst`, `dpsl`, `dpo`, `dpsp`, `dpen`) |
| **Circle Illusion & wobbly walls** | A rhythm-family mode without rings (id `illusion`, the project.jdm "Circle Bounce ILLUSION", "This Circle ILLUSION is WILD", "GET THE WHITE SPACES" and "circle collide circle" formats) in four types: **lines** – N balls (2–32) each slide back and forth on a diameter of the big circle, ball i on the diameter at angle i·π/N with the phase offset i·π/N, so together they form a smaller circle rolling inside the big one (the classic straight-line / rolling-circle illusion; **reveal** draws the hidden circle, **tracks** the diameters) and every ball plays its own C-major degree when it touches the rim; **rings** – 2–16 concentric rings with one ball bouncing between each ring's walls, the rings turning K − k times per cycle, so the bounces ripple outward in spirals and every ball lines up again at the end of each cycle (a flash and an accent); **nested** – 2–5 circles bouncing inside each other with elastic collisions (masses ∝ radius, energy conserved), every layer's wall wobbling where it is hit; **white spaces** – 1–12 balls paint the white arena black while bouncing off invisible shapes they can never enter, so a hidden picture (heart, star, smile, moon, music note, diamond, flower or cross – chosen by the seed, or fixed) is revealed with a chord and a glow once 98 % of the reachable arena is painted, the notes climbing the scale as it fills. Plus **Wobbly Walls** (0–1, the project.jdm "Bouncy Circle") in the Visual section of every ring mode, the Circle Illusion and the Collision Playground's circle container: a wall bulges where a ball hits it – never further than the amplitude – and the bulge travels round the wall and dies away – render-only, sampled at 64 angles per wall from the contacts the engine logs with their simulation time, so it is deterministic, freezes with a pause and replays in recordings. Lines and rings are analytic in the step counter and finish after a set number of cycles (the finder says the length is fixed), nested runs are endless, the white spaces are seeded and searchable by Find Simulation. Settings in the "Illusion" block of the Mode row, shared via the URL (`ilt`, `ilb`, `ilr`, `ild`, `ilp`, `ilpt`, `ils`, `iltr`, `ilrv`, `ilc`, `wob`) |
| **Rigged outcomes** | Guaranteed results for storytelling clips – with a warning in the panel that they are staged. **Find Simulation** gets an **Outcome** select: *Lasts exactly* (the classic run-length search), *Never escapes* (no ball leaves the outer wall for the whole clip – the recording ends with the ball still inside), *Escapes at* (the first escape within ±0.5 s of a chosen second – sync it to a drop) and *Winner* (two balls or more: the chosen team tops the scoreboard when the run ends, or when the clip does – in a battle mode the chosen ball is the last one standing, the battle followed to its end); progress, cancel and the closest run so far work as before, and a found run sets the recording length. The **Rigged Outcomes** group under the Drama Director (advanced options of the Visual section) adds two hard constraints the director enforces on top of the physics: **Never Escape** (`ne`) – every rebound is steered so the next bounce misses the last wall's gap (near misses, which the camera's slow motion picks up), stray flights are bent a little and the wall holds even when a ball reaches the gap, so Classic, Accumulation, Portal, Shatter and Color Match never end – and **Forced Winner** (`fw`, a team) – the other balls cannot get through a wall before the chosen one has (in Multiply they stay in for good), so it breaks the walls first, escapes first and wins the banner. Deterministic (no random numbers: seeds, the finder and recordings replay exactly), invisible in the export (the page says it is on outside the canvas), kept across mode changes and in presets, off by default and a no-op when off |
| **Timeline keyframes** | Automate numeric settings over the clip. A **Timeline** section of the panel picks a setting – gravity, ball speed, ball size, wall rotation speed, gap size, air drag, wind, spin, rotating gravity, wall bounciness, breathing walls or the bumper boost –, sets its value and adds a keyframe at the current simulation time; the list edits every keyframe's time and value or removes it. Between two keyframes a setting glides in a straight line, before the first and after the last it holds. The engine applies the keyframes at the start of every fixed 60 Hz step from the simulation clock (a pause holds them, 8× plays them faster, Restart starts them over), so a run is deterministic whatever the frame rate and Find Simulation replays a found seed with its automation; the automated values reach the run only – the settings keep their slider values, and the sliders of automated settings show the live value, locked, with an **AUTO** badge. A thin bar under the canvas shows a lane per automated setting with a marker per keyframe, a small curve of the value and the playhead. A keyframed gap size resizes the gaps in place (broken rings stay broken). Shared via the URL as one compact parameter (`kf=g_0_300_10_1200*r_0_8_4_20`), saved in presets, kept across mode changes |
| **String Battle (oddplayground)** | A battle-family mode (`stringBattle`, a new **battle** heading on the mode cards) after oddplayground's "WEB DOMINION" reel: 2–6 numbered balls (`sbn`, default 4) bounce inside one thin grey ring on black with soft colour halos, and every wall bounce anchors a glowing thread from the impact point to the ball, so each ball drags a fan of threads (at most `sbm` 3–40, the oldest fading out). Rules (`sbr`): **cut** – crossing an enemy thread anywhere from the ring to its ball snaps it with a pluck pitched by its length and costs its owner a life; **touch** – the threads are lasers that cost the toucher a life; **collide** – the slower ball of every collision loses a life (every ring bounce draws a ball's speed afresh, so the slower one changes from clash to clash); a lost life shields the ball for a moment. A ball at 0 of its `sbl` 1–9 lives (the number on it) shatters like glass with a noise burst and its threads dissolve; when two are left and one is a cut from the end the finale ramps the speed up to `sbf` 1–3× and the halos strobe (toned down under reduced motion), and the last ball standing wins – ring flash, winner banner and confetti, slow motion on the final cut with the camera's slow motion on, the teams banner with a roster (whose colours and names the balls wear; else HOTPINK, AQUA, ACID, VIOLET, SUN, MINT). The WEB DOMINION HUD (`sbh`: a row per ball with swatch, name, kill pips and ×kills, the live threads, a dashed ring around a ball one cut from elimination) and the "FLASHING LIGHTS – THE END GETS INTENSE" badge (`sbb`) are part of the clip (with a roster whose scoreboard takes the top-left corner – HUD off or neon – the badge moves to the top-right one; the teams scoreboard and banner then count **Kills** and the **Win**, not walls and escapes; a ball character's face sits as two eyes above the lives number); the **neon** style (`sbst`) draws every thread as an endless line painted additively into moiré art, wobbles the ring like jelly (`sbw`) behind shivering outline rings and glitches the frame on every lost life. A clip limit (`sbd`) judges the survivors by lives. Deterministic per seed; Find Simulation searches a battle's length or its winner (the last ball standing: every battle is played to its end, and the recording covers it), the Forced Winner rigs it (also with a stale `bi=merge` link: a merge that would absorb the chosen ball hands its identity to the merged one), and a resize mid-battle (a phone rotation) keeps every ball where it was in the ring |
| **Power Layers** | An escape-family mode without rings (id `powerLayers`) after the oddplayground clips "It starts tiny and gets out of control" and "800 layers between the ball and freedom": a navy portrait playfield (the column the exported square shows in full, thin grey frame) with a glowing ceiling bar in its upper third and, from mid-screen down, a dense stack of 20–800 thin rainbow layers (**Layers**, `pll`, default 120; the hue sweeps down the stack and repeats). One white ball (radius 10 by default) bounces between the ceiling and the top of the stack with a seeded sideways **Drift** (`pld`, 0–1); every impact destroys *power* layers – they shatter into particles in their own colours, the band flashes white and the stack top drops – and the power then advances by the **Power Sequence** (`plq`): **double** (1, 2, 4, 8 …, the default), **fibonacci**, **primes**, **+1 per hit** or **chaos** (each power drawn from the seed between 0 and twice the record so far – some hits destroy dozens, others change nothing). Every bounce takes exactly one period (**Bounce Speed**, `plsp`, 0.5–2 bounces a second): the mode solves the speed the ball meets the ceiling with so the up- and down-leg fit the period, so the ball speeds up as the stack drops and the hits land on a steady beat. Two rainbow pills show the rule ("Each hit x2 the power", "fibonacci cooked this run", "prime numbers took over", "every hit +1", "chaos picks the power") with the power and the level and "new sound every level" (**Rule Badges**, `plp`), a corner badge says SOUND ON, FLASHING LIGHTS / THE END GETS INTENSE, both or nothing (**Corner Badge**, `plb`), and "layers left" counts down under the stack; the ceiling bends where the ball touches it (the wobbly-walls wave). Sound goes through the ToneGenerator: each hit is the next degree of the Sound section's scale (C major while it is chromatic), a hit that destroys 10+ layers adds the level's chord and a whoosh (the wall-break sound, which also shakes the cinematic camera), the hit that clears the stack a fanfare (chord + the multipliers' rising arpeggio). When no layer is left the ball falls out of the bottom with a burst – FREEDOM! with the hits and the time – and the run ends 1.8 s later. The run lasts hits × period + 1.8 s, so Find Simulation explains a fixed length for every sequence but chaos and searches the chaos seeds by that predicate; deterministic, analytic flight (the extras never shift a hit); built for 60 fps at 1080×1920 – the whole stack is one pre-rendered blit and the particles are analytic |
| **Fast export** | A ⚡ **Fast export** button under Record Video builds the clip frame by frame instead of recording the screen: a fresh engine replays the current run from its seed in fixed 1/60 s steps, a hidden second instance of the canvas draws every frame with the very same draw routine at the export resolution (sharper than a screen recording, never a dropped frame), WebCodecs encodes it – H.264 in MP4 through mp4-muxer where the browser has an encoder, else VP9 / VP8 in WebM through webm-muxer – and the sound (tones, melodies, hit samples, song slices, the ducked music bed, the characters' chirps) is mixed offline through the same synth code in an OfflineAudioContext and encoded as AAC or Opus. Usually faster than real time, it ends the clip exactly as the real-time recorder does (the run's end, the replay and banner holds, the clip length) and is reproducible: the same seed and settings give the same frames. A progress bar with the frame count and the speed, Cancel, 30 or 60 fps (`xfps`, Recording section); where WebCodecs is missing the button says so and records in real time instead |
| **Project files & short share codes** | **Export project** (a "Project file" block under Saved Presets) downloads `<name>.jumpingballslive.json`: every setting – with the obstacle layout, keyframes, captions, team roster and the mode and arena settings –, the ball emoji and built-in melody, and the media uploaded in this session as base64 (ball image, hit sample, wall-break sound, song-slicer song, background music, custom MIDI melody, Picture Paint picture, background picture), listed with their sizes before the export; above 25 MB of media the panel warns and asks before exporting, above 100 MB a project is refused. **Import project** (or a `.jumpingballslive.json` dropped anywhere on the panel, the sound, MIDI and picture drop zones included) restores all of it – the settings like a preset, then each medium through the page's own upload handlers – after checking the format version (newer files are refused, older ones migrated) and validating every setting (unknown keys dropped, wrong types defaulted, numbers clamped) and medium (base64, size, MIME type). **Short share codes**: the share button copies `?c=<code>` – the link's non-default settings as JSON, `deflate-raw` compressed with `CompressionStream` and base64url-encoded (no escaping, far shorter than the long link for obstacle layouts, captions, rosters and keyframes); a `?c=` link is decoded with `DecompressionStream` on load and its settings are applied before the link's other parameters, which win. The address bar keeps the long, readable link and every long link works as before; browsers without the streams share the long link |
| **Square Racing Grand Prix** | A rhythm-family mode (`race`) for the project.jdm marble-race format: 2–16 racers on the slider (any grid typed, up to `RACER_CEILING` = 256) – squares that tumble as they slide, or marble-like circles – named, coloured and emoji'd from the Teams roster (the first six; the others race in a 16-colour racer palette, then hues a golden angle apart: `racerColor()`) drop from a start grid after a 3-2-1-GO countdown down a long vertical track generated from the seed: rows of pegs, funnels, spinners, pinball bumpers, turbo dash pads, narrow gates and swap zones (the first racer through arms one, the second swaps places with it), `rcTrackLength` screens per lap and `rcLaps` laps (the lap's rows come round again, every lap ending in a swap zone and a turbo strip), the whole mix or one featured obstacle (`rcFeature`). The camera follows the leader or frames the pack (`rcCamera`), a mini-map shows the whole track, the live standings list place, name and gap (interval timing) and slide on every overtake, a pass calls itself out ("Blue passes Red") with a rising chime, every obstacle hit plays the racer's own note, and the winner gets a fanfare, a podium (1st / 2nd / 3rd) and confetti. **Cups** (`rcCup`) keep F1 points across consecutive races in localStorage and show the table after each podium, under an editable title (by default named after the mix: "Turbo Dash Cup", "Swap Zone Cup"…); a **staged winner** (`rcWinner`) is favoured at the swap zones and turbo pads, deterministically. Seeded and timed by Find Simulation, shared via the URL (`rcn`, `rcs`, `rcl`, `rclp`, `rcf`, `rccam`, `rccup`, `rcct`, `rcw`, `rcst`, `rcmm`) |
| **Battle Royale & Capture the Flag** | Two rhythm-family team games of bouncing squares after project.jdm (feature jdm-arena-games; no AI – the drama comes from the physics). **Square Battle Royale** (`battle`): 2–20 axis-aligned squares with hit points (3–20) bounce around a box or a circle; when two collide the faster one deals damage in proportion to their closing speed (× the Damage setting), the square that is hit flashes and shrinks a little, and a square at 0 HP explodes into shards with a "KO!" callout and leaves the fight – the last square standing wins under a banner with confetti; an optional safe zone closes in from the eighth second and pushes the survivors together (never tighter than they need), and heal, shield and speed power-ups spawn at seeded times and places. **Capture the Flag** (`ctf`, "capture the flag 2-2"): two teams of 1–4 squares and a base with a flag at each end – a square that touches the enemy flag carries it (the flag is drawn on it) and scores when it reaches its own base; a carrier tackled by an enemy drops the flag where it is (an enemy picks it up again, its own team sends it home, a flag left lying goes home after 8 s); first to the Captures to Win, else the better score when the clip ends ("TIME!"), with a scoreboard band, a "CAPTURE!" banner and a goal fanfare. Both: elastic square collisions with the physics extras, every square at its own seeded tempo, a Director Nudge (0–1) that turns wall rebounds a few degrees toward the action, names and colours from the Teams roster when one is set (colour names otherwise), faces on the squares, every hit, KO, pickup and capture through the ToneGenerator (instruments, scale snap, beat lock, hit samples, music bed); the speeds scale with the arena, so a seed plays the same on any screen and Find Simulation searches the length of a battle or of a game won on the score. In the "Arena games" block of the Mode row, shared via the URL (`btn`, `bthp`, `btd`, `bta`, `bts`, `btp`, `ctfn`, `ctfw`, `arn`) |
| **Beat Runner & Paddle Keep-Up** | Two rhythm-family modes after project.jdm (feature jdm-rhythm-runner). **Beat Runner** (`runner`, "Added realistic gravity to Geometry Dash"): a square runs along a floor at a constant speed under a sideways-scrolling camera, and the course – rows of 1–3 spikes, pits, blocks to jump onto and drop off – is planned ON THE BEAT of the loaded song (its detected beat, the Picture Paint detector: music bed or song slicer, with its start offset and loop) or of the Sound section's BPM: a jump-timing solver takes the flight time of a jump under real, constant gravity with a fixed take-off speed (from the jump height) – onto a higher block, the same level or off an edge – and times every take-off so the landing falls exactly on a beat (to the microsecond, whatever the frame rate). **Auto Jump** (`rra`, on) jumps at those times and never fails; off, Space (or ↑ / a tap on the canvas) jumps – a mini game where a crash into a block, onto spikes or into a pit restarts the section at its checkpoint a whole number of beats later (ATTEMPT n; Escape pauses). Every landing plays the next note (a scale degree, or the next melody note) with a puff of dust and lights its beat marker; the square rotates in flight and lands flat, squashes, leaves a trail and wears the Character face; a progress bar, the tempo badge and LEVEL COMPLETE! with a chord at the finish. Obstacles 4–120 (`rrn`), speed (`rrsp`), jump height (`rrj`), density (`rrd` – how often an obstacle takes the earliest beat it fits), mix (`rrm`: mixed / spikes / blocks / pits), beat source (`rrbs`: song / BPM). **Paddle Keep-Up** (`paddle`, "Ball Bounce Game with Moving Platform"): a ball falls under real gravity onto a platform sliding left and right; a catch launches it back up – higher from the centre (up to the ceiling), lower and sideways with spin from the edges, the platform's motion adding a little – off the walls and the ceiling, every catch the next note (a chime every ten in a row) and a little faster than the last (`pdu`); **Auto Platform** (`pda`) predicts every landing exactly and heads there, and its **Skill** (`pdsk`, 0–1) makes it plan early or late, aim off and react slower – from the seed, so the misses happen deterministically (100 % never misses); after the allowed misses (`pdm`, the hearts) the next one is GAME OVER. Off, the pointer or ← → move the platform. Platform width (`pdw`) and spin (`pdsp`). Both deterministic, with the Find Simulation (an auto runner's length is read off its plan; a game over is timed on the mode's fast path; a runner played by hand, a hand-played or perfect paddle hide the finder), pause, speed, recording and the fast export |
| **Batch render** | A **Batch** block at the end of the Recording section queues many ⚡ fast exports: **N random seeds** (1–50) or a pasted **list** – one job per line: a seed, a share link (long or short `?c=`), or a link followed by seeds (`https://…?c=… 42 77`; a link without one gets a random seed or its `seed=` parameter; bad lines are named and skipped) –, optionally in **every mode** (a chip per mode, applied like clicking its card: your look, sound and recording settings carry over) or with **one setting swept** from A to B in K steps (gravity, ball speed and size, walls, gap, rotation, air drag, wind, spin, wall bounciness, rotating gravity or the clip length; snapped to the slider). **Render batch** runs the jobs one after another – each job's settings are put on the page, the page renders it with the fast export and gets your settings back at the end, with your uploaded sounds and a run Find Simulation had found; a link's clip keeps this section's resolution and frame rate and your wall-break sound, which links don't carry – with a queue showing every clip's status, progress and time; each clip downloads as `mode-seed-duration.mp4` (plus `-setting-value` in a sweep; optional) and **Download all as ZIP** packs them at the end (a tiny STORE-only ZIP writer, `src/lib/recording/zip.ts`). **Stop after this clip** lets the current clip finish; the fast export's Cancel stops at once. The last batch definition is kept in localStorage |
| **Split-screen races** | Race **2 or 4 arenas at once** on one canvas and in one recording (a 🏁 **Arenas & Split Screen** section of the panel, feature split-screen): **Arenas** 1 / 2 / 4 (URL `ac`; 1 is the classic single view), **Layout** row (side by side) or grid (2 × 2, or two stacked for vertical clips; `al`), **Sound** first arena only (default) or every arena, the others a little softer (`sa`), and an editor per arena – a **label** plus optional overrides of the shared settings: **seed** (the same run on every restart), **gravity**, **ball speed**, **ball colour** and, from the second arena on, the **mode** (compact URL key `ar`, e.g. `ar=Red~cff3366~g600|Blue~s42|~mshatter`). The arenas tile the centred square the recorder exports, each in a world of its viewport's shape with the single view's size (so a seed plays exactly as it would on its own), step together – pause, restart and 1×–8× apply to all – and share one particle budget. Labels with each arena's time and place, and a **race banner**: which arena escaped – or finished – first, and when (every arena timed on its own simulation clock). **Find Simulation** searches a seed for every arena and stores them; the clip ends after the last arena is done. Record Video captures the whole race (Fast export, Batch render and the Viral video bot's render draw one arena, so they are off during a race; the bot plans its clips for the single view); a Beat Runner / Paddle Keep-Up played by hand is played in the first arena, the others play by themselves |
| **Sound Vortex** | A Gerald-family rhythm mode (`vortex`, feature gerald-vortex) after the geraldbounces "sound vortex, pew" clips: a spiral funnel seen from above – 6–24 concentric **sound rings** (`vxRings`) over glowing whirlpool arms, a hole in the middle. 1–30 balls (`vxBalls`) enter at the rim one after another (`vxStagger` seconds apart) with a tangential velocity and spiral inward under a central pull that grows toward the centre plus a light drag, reaching the hole after the **Spiral Time** (`vxDuration`, 3–30 s; the **Central Pull** `vxGravity` sets how many laps they whirl on the way, not how long it takes). Every ring a ball sinks past plays the next degree of the Sound section's scale (ring 0 at the rim is the lowest), so each ball is a rising glissando and the staggered balls weave arpeggios – through the ToneGenerator, so melodies, instruments, hit samples, the beat lock, the slicer and the music bed all work; rings crossed in the same step sound as one chord. The hole swallows a ball with a **pew** – a fast downward pitch sweep synthesised in the ToneGenerator (`playPew()`, in the bounce instrument's waveform, on the beat grid; a chosen wall-break clip plays instead) – and a splash; **Respawn Loop** (`vxLoop`) sends it back to the rim for an endless vortex, and the **Depth Cue** (`vxDepthScale`) shrinks the balls toward the centre and darkens the throat. Deterministic for a seed (the seed's tempo moves the run by ±8 % of the Spiral Time), so Find Simulation lands a clip length; with the loop on the panel says there is nothing to time. The planner searches seeds with the seed finder's engine and predicates so the payoff lands in the last 10–20 % of the clip – or, for the “who won?” ending, 0.5–1 s after it ends –, alternates resolved and cliffhanger endings, fits the length bucket to the platform (Reels, TikTok, Shorts), scores every clip 0–100 against the playbook's checklist and shows **why this clip**; **Today's plan** is the same for the same date, never repeats a recipe two days in a row and rotates the series. **Render all** feeds the plan to the batch renderer and downloads a ZIP with every video, a caption file each (caption, 5–10 hashtags, posting notes), `manifest.json` and `posting-schedule.md`, in English, Polish or Spanish; `node scripts/viral-bot.mjs` does the same headless (`--dry-run` plans in Node) and `--post` / `--post-only` publish Reels through the Instagram Graph API; `.github/workflows/bot.yml` runs it daily |

| **Beat Drop** | A rhythm mode without rings (id `beatDrop`, feature beat-drop) after the "ball lands on the beat" edits: a portrait scene on a dark gradient where one ball drops and bounces from obstruction to obstruction, and for **every beat** of the beat it follows (the beat source in effect – the loaded song's detected beat, the music bed's or the slicer's, an imported video's or audio file's beats or your hand-placed beat markers (Sound section → Beats from a video), the grid Picture Paint and the Beat Runner follow – else the Sound section's BPM) an obstruction flies in from off-screen – left, right, top or bottom, seeded, never from the side the ball comes from – with an ease-out (a back ease for the bouncy kinds) over the **Fly-in Time** (0.3–1 beats, `bda`) and settles a hair before the beat, so the ball lands on it exactly on the beat: a pure planner solves every flight's take-off speed from the interval and the height difference (dy = v·T + g·T²/2), so every landing is on its beat to a fraction of a millisecond at any tempo (50–220 BPM tested) and on irregular grids. Six **Obstructions** (`bdk`): planks, blocks, spring pads (a higher, snappier bounce in the same time, with a boing), wedges (they redirect the drift), spinning bars (flat exactly at the beat) and big drum pads – each squashes on impact, glows with the beat, holds while the ball is over it and slides away with an ease-in and a fade before the ball could come back down through it. **Drift** (`bdd`) walks the ball sideways inside the safe area, **Scrolling** (`bds`) is *endless* (every landing a little lower, a critically damped camera follows it down) or *arena* (it bounces inside the view), **Bounce Height** (`bdh`) sets the apex (the gravity follows the tempo). The ball squashes and stretches (scaled by its speed, restored by a spring), leaves a **Motion Trail** (`bdt`), every landing throws a ripple ring and a particle puff in the pad's colour, downbeats thump the camera (and shake it through the Camera group's screen shake). **Landing Sound** (`bdsn`): synthesised **drums** (kick on beats 1 and 3, snare on 2 and 4, hats on the off-beats, plus the pad's own accent), the next note of the **melody** per landing (the loaded melody, the song slicer or a built-in tune), or **both**; with a music bed the bed leads and the landings accent it. **Obstruction Colours** (`bdc`): per kind, rainbow by beat or the team roster's colours (the ball wears the first team's). Everything is drawn from the plan at the frame's exact time (the engine's clock plus the canvas' leftover accumulator), so the motion is sub-frame smooth at any playback speed, in recordings and in the ⚡ fast export (which plays the same sounds). The run covers the beats of the clip and can never fail – Find Simulation just picks a seed and says how many beats the clip covers; deterministic per seed, resize-proof |
| **Daily challenge & preset gallery** | One mode and one seed a day, the same for every visitor (feature daily-gallery, `src/lib/daily.ts`): the UTC date alone derives the seed (a 32-bit hash) and the mode (the days are dealt in blocks from a pool of 15 modes whose runs end by themselves – every mode once per block, never twice in a row – each with a curated look). A **Today's challenge** card below the hero (the mode's picture, number, date, seed, a countdown to the next one, your streak and today's result) opens `/simulator?daily=1`; the simulator's 📅 **Play today's seed** button (under the canvas) loads it over any setup; `daily=YYYY-MM-DD` opens a given day's challenge (from #1 on 2026-09-30 up to tomorrow). While the challenge is on – its mode, its seed still pinned: a physics change or a new mode ends it – the bar under the canvas names it and the address keeps `daily=<date>`; when its run finishes, a small end-of-run panel shows the run length and the streak (days in a row, kept in localStorage) and copies the challenge link to share. A **Gallery** page (`/gallery`, navbar, footer, sitemap) shows 19 curated presets from `src/content/gallery.ts` – each a canonical share query plus a pinned seed, named and described in every language – as cards with preview images (`public/gallery/<id>.webp`, rendered by `npm run previews`) and a **Try it** link that opens the simulator with the preset applied |
| **Installable offline app (PWA)** | The site installs as an app (Chrome, Edge, Android: the browser's install button or an **Install app** button in the footer while the browser offers it; iOS/iPadOS Safari: Share → Add to Home Screen) and opens in its own window on the landing page (feature pwa). A service worker keeps it working **offline**: the first visit precaches the app shell – the landing and simulator pages in every language, all build files, the mode and gallery previews, melodies, wall-break and hit sounds and the icons (about 7.5 MB) – so the simulator, recording included, runs without a connection; other pages work offline once opened, and a page never opened shows a small offline page in its language. Pages always come from the network when there is one (the cache only answers offline), and every deploy is a new worker version whose caches replace the old ones, so a new release is never hidden behind cached HTML. Production builds only, over HTTPS or on localhost |
| **Bounce math** | Rules that change any value by a mathematical step every time something happens (feature bounce-math) – the "bouncier / faster / bigger on every bounce" edits, generalised: a **Bounce math** block in the Ball & Physics section lists rules of a **parameter** (bounciness, speed, size, gravity, ring spin, gap size, wall thickness, air drag, trail length, colour shift, pitch in semitones, time scale, wall wobble, balls), a **trigger** (every bounce, gap pass, ball-to-ball hit, wall break, **beat** of the song's grid – the loaded song, an imported video's beats or the hand-placed markers, else the BPM –, bar of four beats, second, the start), **every N**-th time, an **operation** (add, subtract, multiply, divide, power, root, modulo, set, seeded random, or a **formula** in v, n, t, b, r with + − * / ^, sin … clamp, compiled by a small parser – never eval), an amount (any value; the slider is a comfort range), optional min / max (no upper limit by default) and this ball / all balls. Six presets (Bouncier every bounce, Faster and faster, Growing ball, Beat pump, Gravity flips every bar, Chaos formula), a live readout and a **Show values** HUD badge; deterministic (the finder, split-screen arenas, the batch renderer and the fast export replay it), in every mode (every mode reports its bounces; the panel marks a parameter a mode ignores and a trigger it never sets off); URL `bmr` (`param.trigger.every.op.amount[.min][.max][.scope]` joined by `;`) and `bmh` |
| **Publish to TikTok, Instagram & YouTube** | A **Publish** block at the end of the Recording section (feature social-publish) sends the last recording, fast export, batch or viral-bot clip – or a picked video file – to **several accounts per platform in one click**: its words per platform (title, caption, hashtags from the viral bot's copy, editable per platform with live counters for each platform's limits: TikTok 2,200, Instagram 2,200 and 30 hashtags, YouTube a 100-character title, 5,000-byte description and 500 characters of tags, #Shorts first), the accounts grouped by platform with avatar and handle, tick boxes and **Send to selected** with per-account progress and result links, and a recent-sends log (the last 50). Three paths, all static-site friendly: **YouTube straight from the browser** (Google Identity Services + the resumable upload API; a client ID from `NEXT_PUBLIC_YOUTUBE_CLIENT_ID` or the App setup panel; any number of channels), **a self-hostable relay** (`relay/server.mjs`, one Node 22 file) that holds the TikTok, Meta and Google app secrets, runs the OAuth sign-ins, keeps refresh tokens and posts – TikTok Content Posting API, Instagram Reels through the Graph API, YouTube – with **access keys** so a team or several creators share one relay, each seeing only their own accounts; and a **no-setup quick share** (the phone's share sheet with the file, or download + caption on the clipboard + the upload page on a computer). The bot CLI posts through the relay too (`--relay`) |
| **Wide sliders** (was No limits) | The **Wide sliders** switch at the top of the Ball & Physics section (`unlimited`, URL `inf=1` – the old No limits key, kept for old links – saved in presets, project files and share codes; off by default) stretches the slider of every uncapped setting past its comfort range on a logarithmic track up to **1B**. It no longer gates anything else: every value is uncapped whether it is on or off (see the Uncapped row), and turning it off changes no value – the track pins at its end. |
| **Uncapped everything** | No parameter has a maximum any more (feature uncap-all, `src/lib/uncap.ts`): every slider in every section has a **number field** next to it (type any finite number – `1e9`, `2.5M`, `0.0001`, `1,5`; Enter, leaving the field or ↑/↓ commit it, invalid text keeps the old value and says why), and the other numeric controls got the same field (the BPM, the clip length, the text size, the rotation speed, timeline keyframes, the batch sweep, the bot, the export frame rate, arena overrides, obstacle rows, Find Simulation's times). A value past its slider pins the track at its end with a faint tint and a "beyond the slider" tooltip, the readout shows it short (1.2M), and links, presets, project files and share codes carry it exactly. The engine runs it as typed – no speed, size, gravity, rotation, restitution or multiplier ceiling – and engages its extreme-values machinery by itself (planned sub-steps and time dilation, time-slicing, the crowd, cheaper drawing) only when a value is past its slider, so at the defaults nothing changes. **Bouncier** is now a numeric **Bounciness** (`bnc`; the old `bounce=1` means 1.03, off = 1): every bounce adds (Bounciness − 1) × the Ball Speed to the rebound, forever – 3, 100, 1e6 – and the canvas shows the fastest ball's speed (**⚡ 12.3K PX/S**) climbing. The only ceilings left are memory-safety ceilings on what a value allocates (a million crowd balls, 10,000 rings, 5,000 entities of a mode…), past which the run says **ARENA FULL**; numbers that overflow the float range are put back at the centre with **NUMBERS OVERFLOWED**. |
| **Windows app (desktop-exe)** | The whole simulator as a downloadable Windows EXE (`desktop/`, Electron): an installer (per user, no admin) and a portable EXE from [GitHub Releases](https://github.com/CronusAztec/Balls/releases/latest), linked from a **Download for Windows** button on the landing page and the `/download` page. Inside the app a **Desktop studio** group appears under the simulator: **GPU** (the graphics card and what Chromium accelerates, the fast export encoding on the GPU first – `hardwareAcceleration: "prefer-hardware"` with the software fallback kept – bundled ffmpeg with NVENC / AMF / Quick Sync picked automatically after a test encode, a benchmark), **Render queue** (seeds, share links or the viral bot's plan × resolutions × frame rates × H.264 / HEVC / AV1 × TikTok / Reels / Shorts / 4K 60 presets, rendered through the fast export and saved straight into a folder – progress, cancel, retry, and a crash-safe journal that resumes after a restart), **AI studio** (a local GGUF model through llama.cpp – downloaded once with its licence shown, resumable and checksummed, GPU offload via CUDA / Vulkan – or your own Anthropic / OpenAI-compatible key: it plans videos with the viral bot and Find Simulation as tools, writes hooks, captions and hashtags, changes settings on request with Undo and pitches ideas from the virality playbook; every reply is validated before it is applied) and **Library** (thumbnails, length, size, play, show in folder, re-render, delete, and one-click **Publish** – to the accounts ticked in the Publish block, or a quick share to the clip's platform). Native menu, tray, shortcuts, file dialogs, drag and drop, single instance and auto-update; the website itself is unchanged |
| **Site design – the stage and the desk** | One calm, dark interface for the whole site (feature site-redesign, see [Design](#design)): semantic colour tokens with a single lime accent, three self-hosted type roles (display, interface, mono readouts), a small component kit in `src/components/ui/`, a landing page with a live 9:16 preview and a modes wall, and a studio in three zones – a rail of control groups, the stage with its readout strip and transport bar, and the panel – with a mode picker dialog and a command palette (Ctrl/Cmd+K). Reading pages share one 680 px column; the gallery is a wall of poster cards. Every setting, URL key, preset, share link and keyboard shortcut works as before |
| **Territory (oddplayground)** | A battle-family mode (`territory`, feature odd-territory) after oddplayground's pong-wars reels ("Vortex VS Bomber – One bounce can flip the board"): a tile map in the exported square – **Board Columns** 12–48 (`tyc`, default 24; the rows follow the board's shape) – split into **2 or 4 team regions** (`tyt`: top / bottom or quadrants), **Balls per Team** 1–8 (`tyb`). A ball passes through tiles of its own colour and bounces off any other, converting the tile it hit with a note pitched by team and row. A **power per team** (`typ`, e.g. `vortex,bomber,painter,ghost`): the **vortex** curves its path and drags a whirl whose spiral arms convert enemy tiles, the **bomber** arms and blows on its next bounce off an enemy tile (shock ring, debris, wall-break sound, the board jolts), taking every tile in its reach – one bounce can flip the board –, the **painter** dashes through enemy territory painting a trail, the **ghost** passes through enemy tiles and converts a 3×3 block on every wall bounce – **Power Every** 1–10 s (`tye`), **Power Reach** 1–8 tiles (`tyr`); the powers are tuned to win about as often as each other on the default board. The HUD band: the FLASHING LIGHTS badge (`tybg`), PICK A SIDE, the countdown (**Countdown** 10–120 s, `tyd`; the clip length follows it), "VORTEX 52% VS BOMBER 48%" and a live percentage bar (`tyh`); an optional dotted grid whose dots deflect the balls (`typg`). The most tiles at the end win (winner banner + confetti, DRAW on a level top); a team roster lends its colours and names, Find Simulation searches a winner, the Forced Winner rigs one |
| **Maze Escape** | A battle-family mode (`maze`, feature odd-maze) after oddplayground's maze reels ("who else thought that was blood"): a seeded perfect maze (6–40 columns on the slider, `mzc`; the rows follow the portrait field) with glowing green walls on near-black, a red entrance slot in the top wall and an exit gap in the bottom wall. 1–8 balls (`mzn`; the Teams roster colours and names the first six) queue in a chute above the entrance and drop in a moment apart, then move physically – a downward pull (`mzg`), perfectly elastic walls, their own speed (`mzs`) – in micro-steps against a cell-indexed wall grid, so no ball ever tunnels through a wall at any speed. A **brain** steers them by nudging the velocity at every cell centre: **bounce** (pure physics), **wall follower** (left, right or alternating hand, `mzh`) or **explorer** (prefers the cells it has not visited – a depth-first search) (`mzb`). Every cell a ball visits is painted in blood red (opacity `mzt`, colour `mztc`, or each ball's own colour `mzto`), every wall hit is a note whose pitch follows the cell's BFS distance to the exit – the melody climbs as a ball closes in – and the first ball through the exit wins (wall-break sound, shake, slow motion as the leader reaches the exit cell, the winner banner with its deadpan caption); the others keep going until every ball is out or the clip limit (`mzd`, then the ball nearest the exit wins). A HUD with a distance-to-exit bar per ball (`mzhud`), the "FLASHING LIGHTS" badge (`mzbg`), fog over the unvisited cells (`mzf`) and the wall colour (`mzwc`). Deterministic: Find Simulation searches the run length or the winner; the rigged forced winner steers the shortest way and the exit stays closed to the others until it is out |
| **Moving exits & splat barriers** | Two options of the ring modes (feature gerald-exit-splat) after the character-ball account's "solving the case of the moving exit" and "Splat Barrier Demo" clips. **Exit Behaviour** (the end of the Wall section; Classic, Accumulation and Multiply – the ring modes with one exit a ring; URL `exit`): **Rotate** (the exits turn with their rings – the default), **Jump** (every ring's exit teleports to a new seeded spot of its ring every `exj` seconds, 1–10, or 0.2 s after a ball comes within `exs` degrees of it – a ball that reaches the doorway first gets out – with a flash where it was and where it lands), **Flee** (the exit runs away from the ball along its ring at up to `exf` degrees a second while the ball is within its sense: the ball has to out-run it) and **Shrink** (the exit narrows over `exj` seconds until it shuts, then opens again at least a quarter turn away). With a moving exit the rings hold still, and the exits move at every physics sub-step, so a gap pass is judged where the exit is at that moment. **Splat Barrier** (Visual section, after Wobbly Walls; every ring mode but Shatter and Color Match; URL `splat`): every wall hit leaves a splat of paint in the ball's colour, with drips, at the impact point on the inside of the wall – `sps` × the ball's radius (0.5–2) – and every splat is a solid circle of the obstacle layer, so the ball builds its own barrier; past `spm` splats (10–300) the oldest fade out, a splat drips off when an exit moves over it or its ring breaks, and when the barrier has kept every ball off the wall for 2.5 s the oldest one falls, so a run never locks up (Grow's splats only paint its ring). A landing plays a wet splat – a noise burst through a falling low-pass over a low blop, or the hit sample an octave down in sample mode. Both off by default and deterministic (the engine's seeded RNG): Find Simulation, links, presets, the fast export and the split-screen arenas replay them |

## Design

The site is built as **the stage and the desk**: the simulation is the stage – the one bright, moving thing on the
screen – and everything else is a quiet desk around it. Near-black surfaces, hairlines instead of boxes, one accent,
no gradients or glows in the chrome, and emoji only where they are content (ball faces, the emoji picker, team emoji,
caption text, the particle previews).

**Tokens** (`src/app/globals.css`, `@theme`): `bg` and three surface steps (`surface-1/2/3`), two hairlines (`line`,
`line-strong`), three inks (`ink`, `ink-2`, `ink-3`), the accent (`accent`, `accent-strong`, `accent-dim`, `accent-ink`
for text on it) and three states (`ok`, `warn`, `danger`). Radii are 6 px for controls, 10 px for panels and 16 px for
the stage frame only; elevation comes from the surface steps plus one soft shadow for dialogs and sheets. Components use
these names (`bg-surface-2`, `text-ink-3`, `border-line`) – never the stock palette or hex values.

**Type roles** (`src/app/fonts.ts`, `next/font/local`, self-hosted from `src/fonts/` with their OFL licences, each in a
latin and a latin-ext subset so Polish and Spanish render in the same faces): **Space Grotesk** for display headings,
**Hanken Grotesk** for the interface, **JetBrains Mono** for readouts – the stage strip, slider values, number fields,
keyboard hints and the uppercase `eyebrow` labels. The `num` utility gives tabular, slashed-zero figures.

**Layout zones and breakpoints**

| Width | Studio | Site |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ≥ 1536 px | rail with icons and labels (240 px) · stage · panel (380 px) | 1200 px container, reading column 680 px with a sticky table of contents on the legal pages |
| 1280–1535 px | icon rail (64 px, labels as tooltips) · stage · panel | as above |
| 768–1279 px | stage, then the rail as a sticky tab strip, then the panel | the reading column alone |
| < 768 px | stage with the transport bar pinned to the bottom; the tab strip is the peek of a bottom sheet that opens to 70 % of the height (a press on the open tab, or on the dimmed stage above it, puts it away and keeps its blocks) | a menu sheet from the header |

The canvas frame is sized from the viewport only (the stage column's width and the screen height), so it changes size
exactly when the window does – the moment the canvas re-measures itself. The world keeps its shapes (square below
640 px, 16:9 above); the export format and its ratio (9:16 by default) are shown in the stage strip.

**Components** (`src/components/ui/`): `Button` / `buttonClass` (primary, secondary, ghost, danger), `IconButton`,
`Chip`, `Segmented`, `Field` with `inputClass` / `selectClass` / `textareaClass`, `Switch`, `Panel`, `Tooltip`, `Dialog`
and `Sheet` (native `<dialog>`: focus stays inside, Esc and the scrim close, the focus returns to the opener), `TabStrip`
(the rail: toggle buttons with roving focus), `Kbd` and the icon set (`icons.tsx`, 20 px grid, 1.5 px strokes). The
studio's own pieces are in `src/components/simulator/studio/` (stage strip, mode picker, command palette); the reading
pages use `src/components/site/ProseLayout.tsx`.

**Motion and access**: 150–200 ms ease-out for panels, sheets and dialogs, nothing bouncy, and none of it with
`prefers-reduced-motion` (the landing preview then shows a still picture). Every interactive element gets the accent
focus ring; hit targets are 32 px at least and grow to 44 px on coarse pointers (the kit's buttons, fields and segments
carry the sizes, and `globals.css` gives every button of the panel the floor – the choice rows of the feature sections
included); the icon rail's labels are drawn in a fixed layer beside the icon (the rail's scroll box would clip them) and
every rail item has its label as a title; the rail and the panel keep the names, `aria-pressed` / `aria-expanded`
semantics and test ids of the old panel headers, and the panel's switches are `role="switch"` named by their labels.
A scroll to the studio (a mode picked, Play today's seed, a bot clip opened) stops below the sticky header
(`scroll-mt-14` on `main#simulator`).

**Adding to it**: a new control group goes into the `sections` list of `Controls.tsx` with an icon from `icons.tsx` – it
appears on the rail, in the tab strip, in the command palette and in the search; a new reading page wraps its text in
`ProseLayout` (an eyebrow, the title, an optional lede and table of contents).

**The command palette** offers what the panel shows in the current mode (`paletteKeyGroups()` in `Controls.tsx`,
`src/components/simulator/panelKeys.ts`): the mode's own block under the Mode group (`MODE_BLOCK_KEYS` – a new mode's
block registers its search keys there), the other groups' controls without the other modes' blocks and without what the
mode or a setting hides (`sectionKeyShown()`: Wall Count outside the ring modes, the arena editor with one arena, a
caption's form without captions …), one entry per label in a group. Choosing a control opens its group and focuses it;
one that its group does not show right now (an advanced option, the collapsed Project file block) is shown through the
search box at once, and a press on the rail always wins over a jump still under way.

## Getting started

```bash
npm install
npm run dev        # http://localhost:3000 (plus the base path, if set) → redirects to your browser's language, /en by default
npm run build      # static export into ./out (+ 404.html and .nojekyll for GitHub Pages)
npm start          # serves ./out the way GitHub Pages does (base path, 404 page, trailing slashes)
```

Copy `.env.example` to `.env.local` to configure the build. Everything is optional for local development:

- `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH` – sub-folder the site is served from (`/Balls` on `https://user.github.io/Balls`, empty for a root deployment).
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` – public URL used for canonical links, hreflang, sitemap and Open Graph, including the base path. Defaults to `http://localhost:3000` plus the base path.
- Feedback channel, first one set wins: `NEXT_PUBLIC_FEEDBACK_ENDPOINT` (an endpoint that accepts a cross-origin JSON POST, e.g. Formspree, Basin or your own worker with CORS enabled), `NEXT_PUBLIC_FEEDBACK_EMAIL` (opens the visitor's email app) or `NEXT_PUBLIC_GITHUB_REPO` (opens a prefilled GitHub issue; needs a public repository with Issues on, and visitors need a GitHub account).
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_ANALYTICS_SCRIPT_URL` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_ANALYTICS_SITE_ID` – load a privacy-friendly analytics script (Plausible, Umami, Rybbit…).
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_YOUTUBE_CLIENT_ID` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_PUBLISH_RELAY_URL` – --- social-publish --- the Publish block's Google OAuth client ID for direct YouTube uploads and the relay URL it suggests (both public; see "Publish").

`next build` reads `.env.local` (and `.env.production*`, `.env`); `npm start`, `npm run smoke` and `npm run previews` read them with
Next's own loader (`@next/env`, in `scripts/dotenv.mjs`), so they see the same `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH` and a base-path build previews correctly at `http://localhost:3000/<base path>/`. You can also pass it explicitly:
`npm start -- --base /Balls` and `BASE_URL=http://localhost:3000/Balls npm run smoke`.

Other scripts:

```bash
npm run typecheck                          # tsc --noEmit
npm run lint                               # eslint
npm test                                   # vitest unit tests (engine, physics extras, ball interactions, obstacles, Ball Drop, Bouncing Shapes, MIDI parser, settings, hit samples, song slicer, scales, instruments, music bed, beat detection, beat clock, Picture Paint)
npm run smoke                              # headless-browser end-to-end checks; build and `npm start` first (see scripts/smoke-test.mjs)
npm run previews                           # regenerate public/modes/*.webp from the real simulator (build and `npm start` first); MODES=drop npm run previews renders one mode
GALLERY=1 npm run previews                 # --- daily-gallery --- only the gallery cards (public/gallery/*.webp); GALLERY=neon-escape renders one
npm run bot -- --count 3 --platform reels   # --- viral-bot --- plan, render and caption today's clips (build and `npm start` first; --dry-run needs neither)
python3 scripts/generate-midi.py           # regenerate the built-in melodies in public/notes
python3 scripts/generate-sounds.py         # regenerate the wall-break and hit sound effects
node scripts/generate-icons.mjs            # --- pwa --- rasterise public/icon.svg into the app icons in public/icons (Playwright's Chromium)
node scripts/generate-og.mjs               # regenerate public/og.png, the social preview image of every page (build and `npm start` first)
```
