  and safe, visible countdown, payoff timing, length bucket vs platform, sound, ending, series, look – each with a reason) and
  the best is kept. `planDay(date, platform, count)` rotates the leading family daily and splits every family into two halves
  that alternate by day, so no recipe runs two days in a row whatever the count; endings alternate clip by clip. The search is a
  generator (`planClipSteps`, `planDaySteps`) so the page runs it in slices.
- **Bot section** – `components/simulator/sections/BotSection.tsx` (search key `BOT_KEYS`, after the Batch block) and
  `components/simulator/useViralBot.ts`: platform, count 1–20, series, length and ending; **Plan clips** (a fresh set with these
  filters) and **Today's plan** (the day's own, the one the CLI and the job make); per clip the recipe, hook, mode, seed, length,
  ending, score and **why this clip**, **Open in simulator** (the settings through the preset loader, the melody, the seed pinned
  like a found simulation), **Re-roll** and **Copy caption**; **Render all** hands the plan to the batch renderer
  (`useBatchRender`'s `runJobs()` – a small hook: jobs with their own settings, seed, name and a `prepare()` that loads the
  melody) and downloads `jumpingballslive-bot-<date>.zip` with the videos (`<episode>-<recipe>-<seed>.mp4|webm`), a caption
  `.txt` each, `manifest.json` and `posting-schedule.md` (`output.ts`). The last plan is kept in localStorage (`store.ts`,
  `jumpingballslive_viral_bot`). Plans are timed for the canvas size they were made in: after a resize the block asks to plan again.
- **CLI** – `node scripts/viral-bot.mjs --count 5 --platform reels --out bot-output [--date YYYY-MM-DD] [--family escape]
  [--bucket standard] [--ending resolved] [--locale pl] [--dry-run] [--post | --post-only]` (also `npm run bot -- …`). With the
  exported site served (`BASE_URL`), it opens the simulator in headless Chromium, plans through `window.__jumpingBallsBot` in
  the page's world, renders every clip with the fast export and saves the videos, caption files, `manifest.json` and
  `posting-schedule.md`. `--dry-run` plans in Node instead (the same planner bundled from `src/lib/bot/node.ts` with esbuild)
  and writes only the text files. It never posts without `--post` (render and post) or `--post-only` (post what `--out` holds,
  after you uploaded it); both need `IG_USER_ID`, `IG_ACCESS_TOKEN` (a long-lived token with `instagram_basic` and
  `instagram_content_publish`) and `BOT_VIDEO_BASE_URL` (the public HTTPS folder Instagram downloads the MP4s from), optionally
  `IG_GRAPH_VERSION` / `IG_GRAPH_HOST` – see `.env.example`; keep them in `.env.local` or secrets, never in the repository.
  Publishing (`src/lib/bot/instagram.ts`, pure request builders and a flow with an injected `fetch`) creates a `REELS` media
  container from the video URL, polls its `status_code` until `FINISHED` and publishes it; a failure names its step, hides the
  token and prints the manual upload steps. Instagram takes MP4 (H.264 + AAC): a Chromium without H.264 + AAC encoders renders
  WebM (Playwright's Chromium on Linux has neither; Google Chrome on Linux has no AAC encoder), so after rendering the CLI
  converts every WebM clip with ffmpeg (on `PATH`, or `FFMPEG_PATH`: `-c:v libx264 -pix_fmt yuv420p -r 60 -c:a aac -b:a 192k
  -movflags +faststart`), removes the WebM and points `manifest.json` and `posting-schedule.md` at the `.mp4`; without ffmpeg
  the WebM stays, with a warning, and is never posted.
- **Scheduled job** – `.github/workflows/bot.yml`: every day at 05:47 UTC and by hand (inputs `count`, `platform`, `post`) it
  installs (with ffmpeg, when the runner lacks it, checked for its libx264 and aac encoders), builds with the Pages base path,
  serves `out/`, runs the CLI (which converts the WebM clips to MP4) and uploads `bot-output` as an artifact kept 14 days.
  It posts only when run by hand with `post` ticked and the `IG_USER_ID`, `IG_ACCESS_TOKEN` and `BOT_VIDEO_BASE_URL` secrets
  plus a `BOT_UPLOAD_COMMAND` repository variable (the command that uploads `bot-output` to that public folder, e.g.
  `aws s3 sync bot-output s3://my-bucket/bot --acl public-read` with `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` / `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` secrets) exist;
  otherwise it warns and the clips wait in the artifact with their posting schedule.
- **Add a recipe** – append a `BotRecipe` to `RECIPES` in `playbook.ts`: `id`, `copyKey`, `family`, `modes`, `payoff`,
  `countdown` (`caption` or the mode's `hud`), `strategy` (how the payoff is timed), optionally `teams`, `character`,
  `musicFirst`, `endings` and `buckets` (what the mode can fill), a `settings(ctx)` generator that keeps every value on its
  `RANGES` step (use `ctx.rng`, `ctx.bucket` and `ctx.payoffTarget`), `hookVars()` and, for words that depend on the settings,
  `copyVars()` (variable → path under `ViralBot`); a mode whose own HUD covers the top of the square goes in `TOP_HUD_MODES`;
  then add its copy under
  `ViralBot.recipes.<copyKey>` in the three messages files (`name`, `series`, `hook`, `payoff`, `cliffQuestion`, `postQuestion`,
  `cliffPostQuestion`, `keywords`, `hashtags`, optionally `cliffHook`). `tests/bot.test.ts` checks every recipe against `RANGES`
  for many seeds, the copy in three languages and the safe zone; update the recipe table above and the playbook's §5.
- **Tests and checks** – `tests/bot.test.ts` (recipes vs RANGES, determinism of planClip / planDay, the square race cut
  before its first finisher, Power Layers' hook vs its sequence, the series label and the arena scoreboard, the daily rotation,
  the score item by item, the safe zone, captions and hashtags per locale, the manifest, caption files and schedule),
  `tests/instagram.test.ts` (the request builders and the publishing flow with a mocked `fetch`), `tests/botCli.test.ts` (the
  options, a real `--dry-run` in Node and the WebM → MP4 conversion with a stand-in ffmpeg); the smoke test plans three clips
  in the Bot block, opens their share links (the canvas' `data-seed` is the planned seed), reloads, opens one, keeps an arena
  game's captions below its scoreboard band, and renders a one-clip short plan into a ZIP it reads back.

### Journey
Feature gerald-journey: a Gerald-family mode in a family of its own (`MODE_CATEGORIES.journey`, the "Journey modes" heading) that strings the other modes' mechanics into one run. The mode is `src/lib/physics/modes/journey.ts`; the stages live in `src/lib/physics/journey/`, one adapter per kind around code that already exists – nothing is simulated twice.

- **Stage interface** (`journey/stage.ts`): `init(bounds, random, ballRadius)` lays a stage out inside its band of the column from its own generator (`stageRandom()`, seeded with one number of the engine's, so a resize before the run lays the same journey out again and one stage never shifts the next), `enter` / `onBallStep` / `update` / `onObstacleHit` / `onBallExit` run it, `render(painter)` describes it to a `StagePainter` – the canvas side (`components/simulator/journeyRenderer.ts`) implements the drawing, so the physics stays DOM-free. `shift()` / `rescale()` move it; `obstacles` are its engine obstacles (drawn by the canvas like every obstacle); `ownWalls` / `reach` tell the journey about a stage that brings its own side walls.
- **Adapters**: `rings.ts` sets the engine's own ring walls (`ctx.setCircularWalls()`) while it is the active stage and delegates the ring hooks to `ClassicMode` – bounces, gap passes that break a ring, the wall tones, the wall-break effects, the cinematic director and breathing walls work as in Classic; the ball drops down a chute into the core, flies under Classic's gravity with the slow-ball boost (`ballsMayRest` follows the active stage), and a medium or large stage bulges out of the column into a chamber (six obstacle walls) so up to nine rings fit (`RING_OUTER`, `MIN_RING_SPACING_RADII`; fewer on a small canvas); after `PATIENCE_SEC` the gaps widen slowly so the stage always ends. `glass.ts` builds panes with Glass Smash's `paneThickness()`, `updatePaneSegments()` and `glassPitch()` and plays them by Glass Smash's own rules, exported from `modes/glass.ts` as pure functions over a `GlassView` that `GlassMode` calls too – `touchGlassPane()` (the contact rule: a touch from above is always a landing), `hitGlassPane()` (the crack and the hop, or the shatter), `spawnGlassShards()` / `stepGlassShards()`, `mapGlassPane()` / `mapGlassShards()` for a resize – so a later change to Glass Smash's rules reaches the journey as well (`glassBits.ts` only gives each stage a Glass Smash view of its own, so `drawGlassWorld()` draws it, and moves it with the floating origin); `pegs.ts` maps `buildDropLayout()`'s rows into the band (bars tilted toward the middle, a C-major degree per row; the outermost peg of a full row sits on the wall as a half-peg and the outermost bar reaches out to it, so no corner at a wall can hold a ball) and reports the narrowest opening of the board – between two pegs, two bar tips (the tightest), or between rows – as its `maxBallRadius()`; `gates.ts` builds rows with `buildGlassGates()` / `gateSlotAt()` and stacks them through the run's `MultiplierRuntime` (a size gate only as far as `maxBallRadius()` of the stages below allows); `funnel.ts` and `bullseye.ts` are built from the obstacle layer (the bullseye's platform is two hinged bars that swing open like a trapdoor, `bullseyeScore()` scores the first landing); `home.ts` is Glass Smash's HOME, played by its `landedOnGround()`, `walkToDoor()`, `reachDoor()` (the chord) and `stepHomeCelebration()` (three confetti bursts, `CELEBRATION_MS`).
- **The mode** keeps one engine ball with `gravityScale` 0 and integrates `journeyGravity()` itself (Glass Smash's gravity scaled to `JOURNEY_GRAVITY`, the speed multiplier squared), so the audio-reactive engine gravity never touches it. Entering a stage shifts the whole world – stages, column walls, the ball and its trail, the engine's particles, shockwaves and obstacle glows (`ModeContext.shiftWorld()`, a floating origin) and the camera – so the active stage's centre is the canvas centre, where the engine centres its rings. Every transition raises the banner (`bannerStage`, `JOURNEY_BANNER_MS`) and queues `{ swoosh: true }`, which the page, the fast export and the split-screen arenas' `playArenaSound()` route to `ToneGenerator.playSwoosh()` (`src/lib/audio/swooshTone.ts`: band-passed noise sweeping up with a 180 Hz → 720 Hz sine glide, never snapped, on the beat grid, ducking the music bed). A ball that rests where no stage holds it on purpose gets a seeded hop after `STUCK_MS` (after `STUCK_SQUEEZE_AFTER` of them in one stage it is set down just below the stage at a seeded spot off the centre line, drifting, so it never lands dead-centre on the next stage's middle peg); the camera eases toward the active stage and never lets the ball out of the field. `JourneyView` (the same object every call) carries the stages, the route, the camera, the banner, the progress, the score and HOME; the canvas mirrors `data-journey-*`.
- **Settings and UI**: `journeyStages` (URL `js`) and `journeyAutoStages` (`jsa`) – `journey/sequence.ts` parses, formats and edits the list (`parseJourneyStages()`, `sanitizeJourneyStages()`, `moveJourneyStage()` …) and draws the seeded route (`generateJourneyStages()`); `resolveJourneyFields()` validates URLs and presets alike; a change restarts the run and drops a found seed. The panel block is `components/simulator/sections/JourneySection.tsx` (Mode row; its keys are pushed into `SECTION_KEYS.ball`); the stage names and canvas words live in the `Journey` namespace of `messages/*.json`. To add a stage kind: add it to `JOURNEY_STAGE_KINDS` (and `JOURNEY_TRAVEL_KINDS`), give it a height in `STAGE_HEIGHTS` and a colour in `STAGE_COLORS`, write the adapter (extend `BaseStage`), create it in `journey/stages.ts`, draw it in `journeyRenderer.ts` (a `StagePainter` method) and name it (`Journey.stage_<kind>`).
- **Tests**: `tests/journey.test.ts` covers the stage list and its edits, the seeded route, the settings / URL / presets, every adapter's layout, the run through the stages in order (banners, swooshes, the floating origin, the camera, HOME), the rings as engine walls, the gates, the glass, determinism, resizes, the finder and the swoosh; `tests/extras.test.ts` holds the fingerprint of the default run. The default route (`rings,pegs,glass,multipliers,rings-s,funnel,bullseye,home`) runs about 30 s, the default clip length. The smoke test checks the card, the panel ↔ URL round trip, a short route at 1× with its swooshes, a found 30 s seed, a 1080×1920 recording and a fast export.
### Bullseye
Feature gerald-bullseye: a Gerald-family mode without rings built on the obstacle layer like Ball Drop (the `"none"` ring layout, the side walls, pegs and bumpers are `obstacles.ts` pieces the engine resolves; `ballsMayRest` + `ballsPassThrough`), so pause, playback speed, restart, the recorder, fast export, faces, emoji and trails work unchanged; the physics extras apply to the flights.

- **Physics** – `src/lib/physics/modes/bullseye.ts`: `buildBullseyeLayout()` lays the portrait field out in the centred square the recorder crops to (the launcher at `LAUNCH_AT`, the deflector band `BAND_TOP_AT`–`BAND_BOTTOM_AT`, the target's centre line – the landing line – at the bottom, its radius `TARGET_AT` × the field width, `MOVING_TARGET_AT` when it moves, its ellipse squashed by `TARGET_TILT`) with `DEFLECTOR_ROWS` staggered rows of candidate pegs and bumpers spaced for the ball size; `chaos` keeps the share with the lowest `hash01(salt, row, column)`, so a seed picks its own field, nested as the chaos grows, and a rebuild at another size keeps the choice. `init()` draws everything from the seed up front (the salt, the moving target's phase, per shot a launch jitter and a triangular aim of ± `AIM_SPREAD` × the field width); shot k leaves at k × `interval` of the world clock, aimed ballistically (`timeToFloor()`). Scoring: `ringAt()` (the ring of a landing by its distance from the target's centre, the bull `bullHalfWidth()` wide, the rim still on the target) and `ringScore()` (10 for the bull down to about 1, `round(10 · (rings − ring) / rings)`). A ball sticks when it reaches the landing line, when it drops onto a stuck ball slower than `STICK_SPEED` (from above) or when it has come to rest on stuck balls for `REST_MS`; one perched on a peg or a bumper is nudged off sideways instead (`PERCH_KICK`, at most `MAX_PERCHES` times – the perfect shot always), a rally longer than `TIRED_MS` tires (`TIRED_DAMPING` per deflector hit) and a gravity below `MIN_GRAVITY` is topped up, so no ball sticks in mid-air; the mode resolves the flying balls against the stuck ones itself (`resolveBallCircle()` against its own circle obstacles, so a pinned ball never pushes itself) and pins the stuck engine balls (`gravityScale` 0) – on the target they ride along with the moving target (`targetOffset()`, a sine of period `TARGET_PERIOD_SEC`). Slow motion: a bullseye starts a `SLOW_MO_MS` window (`slowMoScale()`: a ramp down to `SLOW_MO_FACTOR`, a hold, a ramp back) during which the mode's world clock runs slower and every flying ball's velocity is scaled by the time scale and its gravity by its square – the same path, slower; the launch schedule, the moving target and every animation follow the world clock. The rigging hook: the perfect shot (`perfectShotIndex()`) is aimed at a free spot of the bull (`PERFECT_SPOTS`), steered with at most `STEER_ACCEL` × g sideways among the deflectors and exactly below them (`steerVx()`), flies past the stuck balls (stacking on them when the bull is full) and is held in the bull at the landing – a hard constraint. The run finishes `FINAL_HOLD_MS` after the last landing (`allLanded` raises the final banner); `bullseyeNominalRunSec()` is the summary the panel shows, and the seed moves the length (the last flight, the slow motions), which is what the finder searches.
- **Sound** – pegs return `pegPitch()` from `onObstacleHit()` (degree = the note slot across the field, a diatonic major from C4 while the Sound section is chromatic), bumpers queue the same note accented (and kick the ball sideways, `BUMPER_KICK`), a stuck ball being hit ticks (`stuckPitch()`, at most `MAX_NOTES_PER_STEP` notes a step) – ordinary hits, so melodies, instruments, hit samples, beat lock and the slicer apply. A landing queues a `"hit"` with `thud: true`, `frequency = thudPitch(score)` and a level: `src/lib/audio/thudTone.ts` (`scheduleThudTone()`: a sine body dropping from `bend` × the pitch onto it and a triangle knock above it) through `ToneGenerator.playThud()` – snapped to the scale, on the beat grid without taking a bounce's slot, ducking the music bed, the hit sample two octaves above the thud in sample mode; the page's sound loop, the fast export's `playSoundEvent()` and the split-screen arenas' `playArenaSound()` route `ev.thud` to it. A bullseye queues the race fanfare (`race: "fanfare"` on `fanfareRoot()`), which already goes the way of a bounce.
- **UI** – `components/simulator/bullseyeRenderer.ts` (`BullseyeLayer`): under the balls the bumpers' lime rings, the landing line, the target (dartboard colours `RING_COLORS`, a ring lighting up when a ball lands in it) and the launcher turning toward the next shot and recoiling; over them the score popups and the bullseye's starburst; in screen space the title, `SHOT n/total`, the running total and the big BULLSEYE!; the canvas adds the final banner (the total and the best shot) from the last landing through the hold. The canvas mirrors `data-bullseye-*` (shots, launched, landed, total, best, best shot, bullseyes, scores, notes, thuds, slow motions, time scale, rings, deflectors, moving, target x, perfect shot, all landed, finished) through `BullseyeDataset`. The panel block is `sections/BullseyeSection.tsx` (shots, interval, chaos, rings, moving target, perfect shot and a run summary); a change restarts the run and drops a found seed, the scale and root follow live. Settings `byShots` (URL `bys`), `byInterval` (`byi`), `byChaos` (`byc`), `byRings` (`byr`), `byTargetMoving` (`bym`), `byPerfect` (`byp`), validated by `resolveBullseyeFields()`; the canvas words live in the `Bullseye` namespace of `messages/*.json`.
- **Tests** – `tests/bullseye.test.ts`: settings / URL / presets and the registration, the scoring geometry and the flight maths, the layout (the recorder's square, the chaos selection), a run's launches, landings, sticking, thuds, peg notes and fanfares, deflection off and stacking on stuck balls, the moving target and the balls riding it, the slow-motion profile and the world clock, the perfect shot on every seed (a full field, a moving target), determinism, resizes, the end of the run and the finder landing 30 s, the thud's synthesis and the ToneGenerator's dispatch; `tests/extras.test.ts` holds the fingerprint of the default run. The smoke test checks the card and the preview, the URL ↔ panel round trip and the search, a short rigged run (scores, thuds, the bullseye's slow motion and fanfare, the final banner), the moving target with pause and restart, the finder at 8×, the frame rate of the default run and of a 1080×1920 recording, and a fast export. The card image is `public/modes/bullseye.webp` (`MODES=bullseye npm run previews`; the page's seed is random, so render until the frame catches a BULLSEYE!).

### Beat Drop
Feature beat-drop: a rhythm mode without rings that owns its scene and its motion like Pendulum Wave and the Sound Vortex (the `"none"` ring layout, `ballsMayRest` + `ballsPassThrough`, one engine ball with `gravityScale` 0 whose position the mode sets from planned arcs in `onBallStep()`), so pause, playback speed, restart, faces, emoji, the recorder, the fast export and the batch render work unchanged; the physics extras, the obstacle layer and the ring walls do not apply.

- **Planner** – `src/lib/simulation/beatDropPlan.ts` (pure, no DOM): `planBeatDrop({ beats, beatIndices, period, kinds, drift, scroll, bounceHeight, anticipation, random })` turns the beat times into landings (the beats from `LEAD_IN_SEC` on, each at least `MIN_FLIGHT_SEC` after the one before – a double tap is skipped) in view units (the exported square's side is 1; x from its centre, y down from the first landing), so the plan never depends on the canvas size. The base gravity lifts a flight of one period the Bounce Height (`baseGravity()`: g = 8·h / P²); `flightGravity()` boosts it off a spring (×1.75) or a drum pad (×1.25) and caps it so the apex stays on screen (g ≤ 8·H_MAX / T², a long gap floats); `takeOffSpeed()` solves D = v·T + g·T²/2 for v, with |D| ≤ `DROP_FRACTION`·g·T²/2 so every flight leaves upward and lands descending. Sideways a seeded walk (keep the direction with p = 0.65, Drift × `DX_MAX` × 0.4…1, reflected into ±`X_SAFE`, at most `DX_SPEED_MAX`·T), a wedge sends the ball the way it faces (towards the middle near an edge, at least `WEDGE_DX_MIN`); vertically *endless* drops every landing `ENDLESS_DROP_MIN`–`MAX` lower (past the pad's body when straight below), *arena* steps inside ±`ARENA_BAND`. Each landing's obstruction: a kind from the mix (redrawn with p = `REPEAT_REDRAW` when it repeats), an entry from `allowedEntries()` (never the ball's horizontal side, not the top when it falls straight down), an arrival window of Anticipation × the incoming interval ending `settle` (≤ 50 ms) before the beat – when the landing is higher than the take-off it starts only once the ball has risen past its level –, and a leaving window: it holds while the ball is over it (`clear` = when the ball is past its edge) and is gone before the ball could fall back to its level (`back` = −2v/g), sliding away from the ball (`leaveSide`). Every landing draws `DRAWS_PER_LANDING` random numbers whatever they decide. `sampleBall()` (the exact arc at any time), `padPoseAt()` (offset, angle, alpha and the impact squash – `springDecay()` – of a pad at any time: cubic or back ease-out in, cubic ease-in out with a fade, a spinner's half turns ending flat), `ballDeformAt()` (the impact squash and the speed stretch), `cameraTargetAt()` and `beatEnergyAt()` are what the mode and the renderer evaluate. Drums by bar position: `drumForBeat()` (kick on 1 and 3, snare on 2 and 4), `isDownbeat()`, and a hat half-way through every flight of at least `MIN_HAT_INTERVAL`.
- **Easing** – `src/lib/anim/easing.ts` (shared): clamped cubic / quad / back in and out, smoothstep, `springDecay()` (a damped spring's response) and `criticallyDampedStep()` (the exact critically damped step, independent of how the time is cut).
- **Mode** – `src/lib/physics/modes/beatDrop.ts`: the settings (`BeatDropSettings`, `resolveBeatDropSettings()`, the SimulatorSettings fields and URL keys `bdk`, `bdd`, `bds`, `bdh`, `bda`, `bdsn`, `bdc`, `bdt` through `defaultBeatDropFields()` / `readBeatDropParams()` / `writeBeatDropParams()` / `resolveBeatDropFields()`, and the mode's own 14 px ball through `beatDropModeDefaults()`); **the beat source adapter** `beatDropBeatConfig()` – the one place the mode reads its grid: the beat clock's (the grid it is handed with its offset and loop, else the BPM, through `lib/simulation/beatSchedule.ts`). The page hands it the beat source in effect (feature video-beats: the song, an imported video's beats or the hand-placed markers, resolved by `useVideoBeats.ts` into the `rhythmBeat` the Beat Runner follows; the section's beat line names the source), and the headless finder requests take the markers (`markerBeatInputOf()`). `BeatDropMode` plans the run at init up to `PLAN_HORIZON_SEC` (the seed: `ctx.random()`), places the ball on the arc at every sub-step's time, measures each landing from the ball's state one sub-step before it (`maxErrorMs`), queues the landing's sounds in the step that holds its beat (`landingSounds()`: a `bdDrum` / `bdPad` accompaniment event and/or an ordinary hit for the melody note; `hatSound()` between), kicks the camera and calls `ctx.noteImpact()` on downbeats, steps the critically damped camera toward `cameraTargetAt()` (endless) or the middle (arena), and ends `END_HOLD_SEC` after the clip's last landing (`beatDropFinishSec()`, the clip = the Recording length, live). A resize only relays the view out (`layoutBeatDropField()`), so every landing stays on its beat.
- **Sound** – `src/lib/audio/beatDropTones.ts`: the kick (a sine sweeping 150 → 44 Hz with a click), the snare (high-passed noise and a triangle body), the hat and the six pad accents (a plank's knock, a block's thud, a spring's rising boing with a wobble, a wedge's metallic tick, a spinner's zip, a drum pad's tom) as data and pure scheduling functions; `beatDropVoices()` decides the levels (with a music bed only the downbeat's kick and the accents). `ToneGenerator.playBeatDrop()` plays them on the beat grid when the beat lock is on, snaps the accent to the scale, ducks the bed and never uses up a melody note or a slicer slice; the page's sound loop and the fast export's `playSoundEvent()` route `ev.bdDrum` to it.
- **UI** – `components/simulator/beatDropRenderer.ts` (`BeatDropLayer`): `beginFrame()` takes the engine's clock plus the canvas' leftover accumulator, so the ball, the pads, the camera (its state extrapolated between steps), the trail and the effects are evaluated at the frame's exact time – smooth at any playback speed; `drawBackdrop()` (the dark gradient, parallax beat lines and dust, the focus glow on the beat), `applyCamera()` (the mode's camera plus the Camera group's screen shake, `CinematicCamera.shakeNow()`), `drawWorld()` (every pad alive – drawn per kind with its squash, glow and fade – and the analytic trail), `frameBalls()` + `pushBallSquash()` (the canvas' ordinary ball pass draws the ball – faces, emoji, glow – where the plan has it, squashed and stretched), `drawEffects()` (ripple rings and particle puffs, hashed per landing), `drawOverlay()` (title, tempo, landings, the bar's beats) and the ON BEAT! banner from the clip's last landing through the hold. Nothing is allocated per frame. The canvas mirrors `data-bd-*` (landed, planned, the measured landing times and their beats, the largest error, tempo, pads alive, drums, notes, camera, finish). The panel block is `sections/BeatDropSection.tsx` (the mix chips, drift, scrolling, bounce height, fly-in time, landing sound, colours, trail, the beat it lands on and a run summary that says it cannot fail); a change of what the run is planned from (`beatDropPlanKeyOf()` / `sameBeatDropPlan()`: the mix, drift, scroll, bounce, fly-in and the beat) restarts the run with its music like the Beat Runner, sound / colours / trail / clip / scale follow live. Find Simulation resolves at once (`findBeatDropRun()`): the first seed, the run covering the target's beats. The canvas words live in the `BeatDrop` namespace of `messages/*.json`.
- **Tests** – `tests/beatDrop.test.ts`: the easing curves, the planner landing every beat within 1 ms from 50 to 220 BPM and on irregular grids (tapped, swung, gaps, double taps), flights up and down within the headroom, the safe area, endless / arena, the mix and the fixed draws, spring and drum boosts, obstructions arriving before and leaving after their beat, never from the ball's side and never through the ball, spinners flat at the beat, the squash and stretch, the camera, sounds by beat position and their queueing in the beat's step, the voices, the drum kit through a real ToneGenerator on the fake audio graph and the fast export's dispatch, a 20 s run at 120 BPM whose bounces (measured from the ball's sampled states) are on the beat, determinism, resizes, the clip's end, song grids and re-planning, the renderer's sub-frame ball, settings / URL / presets, the registration and the finder; `tests/extras.test.ts` holds the fingerprint of the default run. The smoke test checks the card and the preview image, the URL ↔ panel round trip and the search box, a 20 s run at 120 BPM landing on the beats (`data-bd-landing-times`), the frame rate at 1080×1920 scaled by the machine's load, a recording that downloads, the finder and a fast export that plays the same drums.

### Rebrand
Change `SITE_NAME` and the accent colours in `src/lib/site.ts`, the theme tokens in `src/app/globals.css`, and `public/icon.svg`
(then `node scripts/generate-icons.mjs`), and regenerate the social preview image `public/og.png` with `node scripts/generate-og.mjs`
against the new build. The pages and messages read the name through `{siteName}`, and the downloads – clip exports, batch and bot ZIPs,
project files – are named after `SITE_SLUG`, its lower-case slug. The accent hex `#93d119` is also written out in Tailwind
arbitrary classes (`text-[#93d119]`, `bg-[#93d119]/10`…) and a few canvas colours across `src/` (globals.css maps it to the
`cyan-600` / `blue-600` / `purple-600` tokens), so a new accent replaces that hex across `src/` too.
These stay fixed on purpose, so a rebrand neither loses visitors' data nor breaks old files: the browser storage keys
(`*_STORAGE_KEY` in settings.ts, daily.ts, raceCup.ts, batch.ts, publish/store.ts, bot/store.ts), the file formats
(`PROJECT_FORMAT` / `PROJECT_EXTENSION` of project files, the bot's `MANIFEST_FORMAT`), the daily challenge's seed salts
(`jumpingballslive:daily…` in daily.ts – new salts would change every day's challenge) and internal ids nobody sees (the
`jumpingballslive:select-mode` event of the mode cards, the publish relay's `RELAY_NAME` message source).
The Windows app (`desktop/`) carries the name on its own: `productName` / `appId` in `desktop/electron-builder.config.cjs`, the
window, tray and menu titles (`desktop/src/main.ts`, `desktop/src/menu.ts`), the default output folder `Videos\<name>` (`main.ts`;
the Render queue names it through `{siteName}`) and the portable data folder (`desktop/src/paths.ts`).
`SITE_DOMAIN` – the address the legal pages name the site by – is not hard-coded: it is `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` without the scheme
(`cronusaztec.github.io/Balls` on GitHub Pages), so a custom domain set in the Pages settings (which `deploy.yml` turns into
`NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`) shows up there on the next deploy; `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_DOMAIN` overrides it. The footer shows the site name
and exported clips carry no watermark unless the user types one.

### Beats from a video
Feature video-beats: "make it understand a video and have the ball land on the beat – or let me put the beats myself – and do it automatically". One beat-source layer that every rhythm feature consumes, two new sources (a media file and hand-placed markers) and On beat for the ring modes.

- **The beat source** – `src/lib/simulation/beatSource.ts` (pure, `tests/beatSource.test.ts`). One small interface other features build on (documented in the file header): `BeatSourceGrid { kind: "bpm" | "song" | "media" | "manual", beats, downbeats, bpm, offset, energy, duration, loop }`. `bpmBeatSource()`, `analysisBeatSource()` (the detector's grid of `lib/audio/beats.ts`, for the song or the media) and `manualBeatSource()` build it; `beatClockConfigOf()` turns any grid into the `BeatClockConfig` that `beatClock.ts` and `beatSchedule.ts` already read (song time = the music bed's start offset + simulation time, wrapped when the bed loops), so "when is the next beat" has one answer everywhere. `nextGridPointSec()` / `previousGridPointSec()` / `gridErrorSec()` add subdivisions (1/4 beats, 1/8, 1/16), `beatEnergies()` + `downbeatPhase()` guess the downbeat from the loudest bar phase, `waveformPeaks()` feeds the strip. The markers are milliseconds: `serializeMarkers()` / `parseMarkers()` write them delta-encoded with runs (`250.500*63` is a steady 120 BPM grid; URL `bm`), `addMarker` / `removeMarker` / `moveMarker` / `nudgeMarkers` / `snapMarkersToOnsets` / `halveMarkers` / `doubleMarkers` / `steadyMarkers` edit them, `estimateTapTempo()` fits taps (median interval, then least squares; a skipped beat counts as two, a pause of 2.5 s starts over).
- **Who follows it** – `components/simulator/useVideoBeats.ts` resolves the picker into the page's `activeBeats` (the grid Picture Paint and the Beat Runner / Paddle Keep-Up were already reading): exactly the old song grid for **Song**, the media's or the markers' grid for **Media** / **Manual**, none for **BPM** (they follow the BPM then). A picked source without its input (no media, fewer than two markers) falls back to the song, else the BPM, and the panel says so. The beat lock follows a media or manual grid too: `ToneGenerator.setBeatSourceClock()` delays a sound to the grid's next point on the simulation clock (subdivided by the grid setting); with the BPM or a song it keeps its old BPM grid. Split-screen arenas get the page's On beat grid too, and the bot's headless finder requests carry On beat from the settings (`onBeatConfigOfSettings()`: the hand-placed markers or the BPM – a page's song is not there). **Beat Drop** follows the same `rhythmBeat` as the Beat Runner, so its obstructions land on the media's beats or the markers (its beat line says which), and the headless requests give the Beat Runner and Beat Drop the markers too (`markerBeatInputOf()`); the video background is drawn over Beat Drop's own backdrop.
- **Media import** – `src/lib/audio/mediaImport.ts`: `decodeMediaFile()` reads the bytes with `decodeAudioData` (Chromium, Firefox and Safari demux the audio of MP4 / MOV / WebM this way); a container the decoder refuses plays through a hidden media element into a `MediaElementAudioSourceNode` and a ScriptProcessor copies the samples out – at 2× with `preservesPitch = false` where the browser allows it (the track is then read back at half the context rate: fine for beats and a background bed, the top octave is lost), else in real time – with a progress bar and Cancel. Limits: files up to 100 MB (the project files' cap); a codec the browser cannot play (or DRM) fails with a message; a file without sound has no beat. `analyzeMedia()` runs the detector in idle slices, then the beat energies, the downbeat and the waveform. The decoded track becomes the **music bed** (volume, ducking, loop, start offset all apply, and the recorder and the fast export carry it); loading another track in Background music, or removing it, drops the media. The file is kept for **project files** (asset kind `beatMedia`), never in links.
- **Video background** – `components/simulator/videoBeatsRenderer.ts`: a hidden muted `<video>` kept on the song time (it plays along while the run goes, re-seeks past 0.2 s of drift, rests on the exact frame while paused) and drawn cover-fitted over the theme background at `videoBgOpacity`. The fast export seeks it before every exported frame (`FastRenderOptions.beforeFrame`); `prepareExport()` times four seeks first and leaves the layer out with a note when a seek takes longer than 150 ms (Record Video then includes it).
- **Markers editor** – `components/simulator/sections/VideoBeatsSection.tsx`: the waveform is drawn once per file into an offscreen canvas and every redraw copies the visible window (zoom and scroll) and draws the detected beats, the onsets, the markers (the downbeats amber) and the playhead. Click to add, click a marker to remove it, drag to move it; ▶ previews the file; **Tap** (or **T** while the preview or the run plays) collects taps, **Use taps** writes a steady grid; **Use detected beats**, **Snap to onsets**, ½× / 2×, ±10 ms, the downbeat (auto / 1–4) and **Clear** edit the markers, which become the **Manual** source.
- **On beat** – `src/lib/physics/onBeat.ts` (`OnBeatController`, wired into `engine.ts` in delimited `--- video-beats ---` blocks: `setOnBeat()` / `getOnBeatStats()`, a contact note at every rebound and gap pass, one call after each 60 Hz step, a reset per run). After a contact it predicts the next one by replaying the engine's own sub-step integration for that ball (gravity and its direction, wind, drag, the cruise boost) against the intact rings, picks the grid point to land on – a whole beat when one is reachable, else the grid setting's subdivision – and retimes the flight: velocity × c, the ball's gravity × c², which keeps the path and changes only the speed along it, within the **On beat range** (×1.5 … ÷1.5 by default); a secant iteration on the prediction makes c exact, the flight is re-checked every few steps (every step near the target, at once after another ball knocks it), and a fresh rebound resets the ball to its natural speed. Contacts are stamped with their step's time and aimed at the step's middle, so a timed hit lands within half a step (8 ms) of its grid point; a hop no grid point can reach within the range flies free. No position changes, no random numbers: a run is fully determined by the seed, the settings and the grid, so the page, the fast export and the finder replay it identically (`ModeSettings.onBeat`), and a found run reports the beats its hits cover (`beatCoverage()`). The rigged constraints act on the retimed flights like on any other. It applies to the ten ring modes (not while Picture Paint's own beat sync moves the ball) and stands aside without a tempo. The canvas mirrors the statistics onto `data-onbeat-*` (timed hits, on beat, worst error of the last 16, beats covered, free flights).
- **Settings** (`src/lib/simulation/videoBeatsSettings.ts`): `beatSource` (URL `bsrc`, default `song` – the old behaviour), `beatMarkers` (`bm`), `beatDownbeat` (`bdb`, −1 = auto), `onBeat` (`onbeat`), `onBeatRange` (`obr`, 0.1–0.8), `videoBackground` (`vbg`) and `videoBgOpacity` (`vbgo`); they carry over to another mode like the music bed and reset with the Sound section.
- **Tests** – `tests/beatSource.test.ts` (a click-track WAV decoded to a grid whose beats sit within 5 ms of the clicks, the accented downbeat, the waveform, tap tempo, the marker text round trip and editing, switching sources keeps the schedule, subdivisions and errors, the settings round trip) and `tests/onBeat.test.ts` (timed hits on the grid, an irregular hand-placed grid, determinism, the untouched default path, path-preserving retiming and release, ring modes only, the finder's engine and coverage, the beat lock on a media grid); the smoke test imports a generated click track, checks the detected BPM and the markers, turns On beat on and reads the hits' error off the canvas, and records a clip with its audio.

### Split-screen races
Feature split-screen: 2 or 4 arenas on one canvas and one recording, without touching the single-arena code paths.

- **Settings** – `src/lib/splitScreen.ts` (pure): the fields `arenaCount` (1 | 2 | 4, URL `ac`), `arenaLayout` (row | grid, `al`), `soundArena` (first | all, `sa`) and `arenas` (per-arena `{ label, seed?, gravity?, ballSpeed?, ballColor?, mode? }`, the compact URL key `ar` – arenas split by `|`, fields by `~`: `s` seed, `g` gravity, `v` ball speed, `c` colour, `m` mode) with defaults, `SPLIT_SCREEN_RANGES`, validation (`resolveSplitScreenFields()`, URLs, presets and project files alike) and `splitScreenCarryOver()` for a mode change; the override merge (`mergeArenaSettings()`: the shared settings with an arena's gravity, speed, colour and – not for the first arena, which always plays the Mode picker's mode – its mode on top; `resolvedArenas()` fills labels and missing arenas; `patchArena()` / `withArenaSeeds()` edit them); the viewport maths (`arenaViewports()`: the arenas tile the centred square the recorder crops, in reading order; each arena's engine simulates a world of its viewport's shape whose shorter side is that square's side – a single arena's world – drawn scaled by `scale`); `splitRestartKey()` (a new count, seed or mode restarts every arena; gravity, speed and colour follow live) and the race (`raceStandings()`: each arena timed by its first escape, else its finish, on its own simulation clock; ties on the same step).
- **Engines** – `src/lib/simulation/multi.ts` (`MultiArenaRunner`): the first arena is the page's own engine (Simulator.tsx lays the first arena's gravity / speed / colour / seed over the settings); the runner keeps one more `PhysicsEngine` per arena, set up through the page's hooks exactly like the page's engine and the fast export's (`arenaPhysicsConfig()` + `initEngineForMode()`), follows every settings change (from scratch before the run starts, only what changed during it), notices the page's engine restarting (its clock going back or another mode) and restarts the others on the same frame, shares the particle budget out (`engine.setParticleBudget()`, `arenaParticleBudget()`), drains the other arenas' sound queues every frame (played through `playArenaSound()` like the page plays its own – bounces at `EXTRA_ARENA_LEVEL`, `melody: false` accompaniment kept – only with "every arena"; their wall breaks are counted either way for the faces, `takeWallBreaks()`), marks each arena's first escape (`engine.getFirstEscapeMs()`) and finish (`engine.getFinishedAtMs()`: the step the run finished on, which the engine records in `update()` – exactly the finder's run length, however many steps an 8× frame runs) and holds the finished race `SPLIT_FINISH_HOLD_MS` before the end screen. The page's hooks (`ArenaHooks` in Simulator.tsx) let the other arenas play a hand-played Beat Runner / Paddle Keep-Up by themselves (the player's keys and pointer drive the first arena). `findArenaSeeds()` wraps the finder: the first arena is searched as always, then every other arena with its overrides and world (`arenaFinderRequest()`, progress tagged with the arena searched – `data-search-arena` on the progress bar); the seeds go into `arenas[i].seed`, the clip lasts the longest run. A search aborted at any point – Cancel, a mode change, a preset load – is never found, even when the first arena's search already was, and `runFinder` applies nothing of an aborted search (its settings may be stale).
- **Canvas** – `src/components/simulator/splitScreenCanvas.tsx` wraps the canvas component (`withSplitScreen()`, around `withFastRender()` in `Canvas.tsx`): with one arena it renders the canvas as before; in a race every arena is drawn by its own hidden instance of the canvas – the unchanged single-arena draw routine, with every prop the page gives the canvas – in the fast export's offline mode (a driver with the arena's world, its viewport's scale in device px and a clock from its first frame), and one animation loop composes them into the visible canvas (`drawImage()` into each viewport) with borders, the arena labels (time and medal once an arena is done), the race banner, the watermark, the Top / Bottom Text and the captions (on the first arena's run); the Grand Prix cup table is left to the first arena, whose race the page scores. It implements the canvas handle (recording, end-screen holds, background of the export's letterbox) and mirrors `data-split-*` for the smoke test.
- **Panel** – `src/components/simulator/sections/ArenasSection.tsx` (the "Arenas & Split Screen" section: count, layout, sound, one editor per arena); messages under `Controls.split*` and the `SplitScreen` namespace (the banner).
- **Tests** – `tests/splitScreen.test.ts` (viewport maths, the override merge, URL / presets, the standings, the runner with real engines – an arena replays the finder's run of its seed exactly and its finish mark is the finder's run length at 8× –, the sounds and wall breaks of the other arenas, and the finder wrapper); the smoke test races four arenas, records a race, finds seeds for two and cancels a search – or changes the mode – while the second arena is searched.

### Daily challenge and preset gallery
Feature daily-gallery: a challenge a day and a page of curated presets, both built on the share links the simulator already reads.

- **Daily challenge** – `src/lib/daily.ts` (pure, `tests/daily.test.ts`): `dailyChallenge(date)` → `{ date, number, seed, mode, query }`. The
  seed is `1 + hash32("jumpingballslive:daily:<date>") mod (2³¹ − 2)`; the mode comes from `DAILY_POOL` (modes whose run ends by itself and depends
  on the seed, each with a curated look as link parameters), dealt in blocks of `DAILY_POOL.length` days from `DAILY_EPOCH` – every block a seeded
  shuffle, a block that would start with the previous block's last mode swaps its first two days. **Shared `daily=` links depend on this
  derivation**: appending a mode to the pool reshuffles the blocks from the one it lands in, and changing the hash, the epoch or a look changes
  every challenge (the test freezes a few days). `dailyFromParam()` reads the link parameter (`1` = today, a date from #1 to tomorrow), history
  and streak helpers keep the first result of each day in localStorage (`jumpingballslive_daily`).
- **Simulator** – `src/components/simulator/useDailyChallenge.ts`: a `daily=` link's settings are the page's first settings (Simulator.tsx's
  initial state) and its seed is pinned once the engine is up (`pinSeed`, after the page's own seed effects); the button loads the settings
  through the preset loader, waits for the commit (whose effects drop any pinned seed) and pins the seed. The challenge counts as on while the
  engine still has its seed pinned in its mode (`engine.getPinnedSeed()`) – every physics change unpins a seed, so tweaking the physics ends it
  and a new colour does not; while on, the address carries `daily=<date>` on top of the mirrored settings. `DailyChallengeUi.tsx` holds the bar
  under the canvas and the end-of-run panel (outside the canvas drawing, so recordings never show it); `formatRunSeconds()` of the engine's
  finish step is the result.
- **Landing card** – `src/components/site/DailyChallengeCard.tsx`, below the hero; it reads the visitor's clock after mount (the page is static).
- **Gallery** – `src/content/gallery.ts` (`tests/gallery.test.ts` checks every query is canonical, has a seed, a preview image and copy in every
  language) and `src/app/[locale]/gallery/page.tsx` (cards with `data-gallery-*` attributes, an ItemList JSON-LD). To add a preset: build it in
  the simulator, copy the address bar's query, add `seed=` (the canvas' `data-seed` or a found simulation), append an entry with names and
  descriptions in every language, build, serve and run `GALLERY=<id> npm run previews` – the script reads the cards from the served
  `/en/gallery/` page and renders each card's query at its `previewAt` second.
- **Copy** – the `Daily` and `Gallery` namespaces in `messages/*.json` (the navbar and footer links use `Gallery.navLabel`); the smoke test opens
  the gallery, Try it, the landing card, `daily=` links, the button, a finished daily run and its share link.
### Installable offline app (PWA)
Feature pwa: a web app manifest, icons and a hand-written service worker (no library), all static.

- **Manifest** – `src/app/manifest.ts` renders `out/manifest.webmanifest` from `buildWebAppManifest()` in `src/lib/pwa.ts`: name (`SITE_NAME`), the English description, `start_url` `<base>/en/`, `scope` `<base>/`, `display: standalone`, the theme and background colour (`PWA_THEME_COLOR`, `PWA_BACKGROUND_COLOR` – the page background) and the icons: `public/icon.svg` plus the PNGs in `public/icons/` (192, 512, a 512 maskable one with the artwork inside the safe zone and a 180 apple-touch-icon), rendered from `icon.svg` by `node scripts/generate-icons.mjs` with Playwright's Chromium – run it again after changing the icon and commit the PNGs. Both root layouts link the manifest, the apple-touch-icon, the iOS web-app title and `theme-color` through their `metadata` / `viewport`.
- **Service worker** – `scripts/pwa/sw.template.js` is plain JavaScript; `scripts/postexport.mjs` calls `buildPwa()` (`scripts/pwa/build.mjs`), which writes `out/offline.html` (a self-contained page: inline styles and icon, the `Pwa` strings of every `messages/*.json`, in the language of the URL it answers for) and `out/sw.js` = the template + its configuration: the **precache list** (the root, the offline page, the landing and simulator pages of every locale with their RSC payloads, every `/_next/static` file and every public asset except `og.png`, `robots.txt`, `sitemap.xml` and files over 4 MB; `selectPrecache()`), the **version** (a hash of every exported file and the template, `hashExport()`) and the cache prefix (the `package.json` name). Cache names are `<prefix>:<scope path>:<version>`, so sites sharing a `github.io` origin never touch each other's caches.
- **Strategies** – navigations, `*.html`, the RSC payloads (`*.txt`), the manifest and other data: network first (the answer – also a 404 – is returned and, when OK, cached; the cache answers only when the network fails, then `offline.html` for a page never cached; navigation preload on); `/_next/static` and the public assets: cache first. Cross-origin requests, non-GET requests, byte ranges and `sw.js` itself are left to the network. Cache keys drop the query (`?mode=…&seed=…` share links open the cached simulator offline) and percent-encode every path segment one way (`[locale]` = `%5Blocale%5D`).
- **Updates** – install precaches past the HTTP cache (`cache: "reload"`; unchanged content-hashed `/_next/static` files are copied from the previous version's cache), fails as a whole if a file is missing (the next page load retries) and calls `skipWaiting()`; activate deletes this scope's caches of other versions and `clients.claim()`s the open pages. `src/components/site/PwaRegister.tsx` (rendered in both root layouts) registers `<base>/sw.js` with scope `<base>/` and `updateViaCache: "none"` after the page has loaded – only in production builds and only over HTTPS or on localhost (`canRegisterServiceWorker()`); in development it unregisters a worker a production build left on the same scope. A tab that stays open checks for a new version when it becomes visible again (at most every 30 minutes); nothing ever reloads the page by itself, so a running simulation or recording is never interrupted.
- **Install button** – `src/components/site/InstallAppButton.tsx` in the footer keeps Chrome's `beforeinstallprompt` (`installPrompt.ts`) and shows **Install app** while the browser offers the install. Strings: the `Pwa` namespace of `messages/*.json`.
- `localeAlternates()` lives in `src/i18n/alternates.ts`: a layout may only export Next's own fields, and the build type-checks the layout whenever webpack keeps it in a page's chunk.
- **Tests** – `tests/pwa.test.ts` (the manifest, the registration rules, the precache list, the version hash, the offline page, `buildPwa()` on a fake export, and the worker itself in a fake worker scope: precaching, the caches of other versions and scopes, network first / cache first, the offline fallbacks, a failed install and a new deploy replacing the old cache); the smoke test checks the served files and head tags, the registration and the precache, then, through a local proxy that can drop every connection, runs the simulator offline, opens the offline page in Polish and simulates a new deploy (every page marked, a new `sw.js` version): the page comes from the network, the new worker replaces the old cache and serves the new pages offline.
### Territory (oddplayground)
The oddplayground family's pong-wars mode (feature odd-territory): `src/lib/physics/modes/territory.ts` holds the settings, the pure board maths and the `TerritoryMode` plugin; `src/components/simulator/territoryRenderer.ts` draws it and `src/components/simulator/sections/TerritorySection.tsx` is its block of the Mode row; `tests/territory.test.ts` covers them (the board, the rule, its symmetry and order independence, every power, the percentages, the verdict, the rig, determinism, a resize, the finder).

- **Board**: the mode owns its playfield (the `"none"` ring layout). `territoryField()` puts a grid of `cols` × `territoryRows(cols)` tiles (the rows follow the field's shape, rounded down to an even count) in the centred square the recorder crops to, under a HUD band (`TY_HUD_BAND` of the square). The tiles are a `Uint8Array` (row-major, the owner per tile) with running counts per team; `regionOwner()` / `fillRegions()` split it into halves (2 teams) or quadrants (4 teams, an odd middle column shared row by row), every team within one tile of the others.
- **Rule**: the engine moves the balls (no gravity, `ballsMayRest` – the mode keeps each ball at its cruising speed, a fraction of the square per second, so a run looks the same on any canvas) and resolves ball-to-ball collisions in its pair loop; `onBallStep()` bounces a ball off the frame (a seeded scatter, headings kept off the axes: `awayFromAxes()`), off the pegs (`TY_PEG_STEP`, through the obstacle layer's `resolveBallCircle()` with a reused peg) and runs the pong-wars probe: eight points on the ball's rim; every probe moving into a tile of another team converts it (`convert()`), and the ball reflects once about the sum of those probes' directions (a flat border sends it straight back, a concave corner back the way it came). A ball whose centre sits on an enemy tile (a blast engulfed it) converts that tile, so it eats its way out; `onPostSubStep()` keeps a ball pushed by a collision on the board.
- **Fairness**: nothing depends on which side of the board a team plays or on the order the engine moves the balls in. The reflection is the same for every heading (reflecting at each hit in a fixed probe order favoured one side by a few tiles a run – enough to decide most crowded battles); the conversions of a sub-step are queued and applied in `onPostSubStep()` (`flush()`, `apply()`: counts, the flip log, a note), so every ball judges the board as it stood when the sub-step began; and the balls join in rounds – a ball of every team a round, the team that starts a round turning from a seeded first one –, so no team always moves first. The powers are tuned on the default board (24 columns, 2 balls a team): the vortex, the bomber and the painter win between about two and three fifths of their pairings; the ghost, which never fights at the borders, is the underdog of a two-team battle (it takes a fifth to two fifths of them, less on big boards, where its 3×3 blocks are a smaller share) and the favourite of a four-team battle of single balls, where most of the frame it bounces off is enemy ground.
- **Powers** (`TyPower`, one per team slot): timed ones fire every `powerEvery` seconds from a seeded phase per ball – the **vortex** (always curving at `TY_VORTEX_CURVE` rad/s) drags a whirl whose two spiral arms (`whirlPoint()`) sweep out to `radius` tiles over `TY_WHIRL_MS`, converting the tiles they land on; the **bomber** arms (`armedMs`) and blows on its next bounce off an enemy tile – or where it is when no such bounce comes within `TY_ARM_MAX_MS` –, converting every tile within `radius`, pushing nearby balls away, logging a shock (`shocks`) and queueing a `"gap"` sound (the wall-break sound, which is also the camera's screen-shake event); its next charge starts at the blast; the **painter** dashes `TY_DASH_TILES` tiles (the same trail on any board) without bouncing off enemy tiles, converting the tile under it. The **ghost** (a faster ball) never bounces off or converts enemy tiles and converts a whole 3×3 block on every frame bounce (the block around its tile, moved inside the board).
- **Sound**: every sound is an ordinary engine sound event, so melodies, instruments, hit samples, the beat lock and the music bed work: a flip is a `"hit"` pitched by team and row on a pentatonic ladder (`flipFrequency()`, at most one per step and `TY_NOTE_GAP_MS`), a dash or a ghost block an accented hit, a whirl the multiplier arpeggio, a blast the wall-break sound.
- **Verdict**: the last `TY_FINALE_MS` ramp the speed up (`territoryFinaleFactor()`) and a lead change there asks the camera for slow motion (`noteNearMiss`). When the countdown runs out the board freezes: `territoryLeaders()` names the teams with the most tiles, every team is credited with its tiles as "walls" and the leaders with an "escape" – so the teams banner, the finder's "winner" outcome and the scoreboard rank the battle by tiles, and a level top stays a DRAW. The mode is in `BATTLE_WINNER_MODES`: the rigged forced winner is enforced by the mode (`rigBlocksConversion()` absorbs a conversion that would put any rival ahead of the chosen team; a level verdict goes to it), and the finder follows every run to its end. Every run lasts its countdown whatever the seed (`fixedRunDurationSec()`), so the finder's useful search is the winner – and that search follows each run to the countdown's verdict however far past the clip and the search's horizon it lies (`simulateOutcomeRun()` stretches the horizon to the countdown + `FIXED_RUN_SLACK_MS`; the page's horizon, Find Duration + 30 s, used to cut a 61–120 s countdown short, and no seed could match).
- **Canvas**: `TerritoryLayer` keeps the tile map in an offscreen canvas (device pixels) and repaints only the tiles whose owner changed (a byte diff against the copy it painted), then draws the pops of fresh flips (a springy scale from the wobble field's `wobbleEnvelope()`), the dotted grid, the frame, the blasts (shock ring, seeded debris) and the whirls under the balls; soft halos and glossy bodies per team (a ghost translucent, a charge ring before a timed power, a flickering ring around an armed bomber) in the ball pass; a blast jolts the board (`blastJolt()`: the camera's shake envelope and wobble at `TY_BLAST_SHAKE`, on the simulation clock, applied by Canvas.tsx to the world view while the camera's own Screen Shake is off – with it on, the camera shakes the view on the blast's wall-break sound); and the HUD band (badge, PICK A SIDE, countdown, "VORTEX 52% VS BOMBER 48%" – a team's power or, without one, its name –, the eased percentage bar) plus, without a roster, the winner banner and confetti in screen space. `writeTerritoryDataset()` mirrors the state onto `data-ty-*` attributes (`data-ty-jolts` counts the blasts that jolted the board). With a team roster, `TeamLayer` pads it with the palette (PINK, CYAN, LIME, GOLD), hides the scoreboard while the HUD shows (without the HUD the scoreboard's columns read TILES – the live count – and WIN, and the badge moves to the top-right corner when the scoreboard sits top-left) and draws the winner banner. `drawOverlay()` returns the bottom of the HUD band (or of the badge), which the top captions start below. Tile bounces, frame bounces and peg hits are bounce math's "bounce" trigger (`ctx.noteBounce()`), ball-to-ball hits its "ball hit" and a bomber's blast its "wall break".
- **Settings**: `tyCols` (`tyc`), `tyTeams` (`tyt`), `tyBallsPerTeam` (`tyb`), `tyPowers` (`typ`, a comma list of the four slots), `tyPowerEvery` (`tye`), `tyRadius` (`tyr`), `tyDuration` (`tyd`), `tyPegs` (`typg`), `tyBadge` (`tybg`), `tyHud` (`tyh`); a change of the fight restarts it and drops a found seed, the badge and the HUD follow live. The mode's default clip covers the countdown and its verdict (`territoryClipSec()`), and the Countdown slider moves the clip with it. Every number is uncapped (see Uncapped everything): a value typed past its slider runs as typed – a reach of 50 tiles, a power every 30 s, a ten-minute countdown, whose clip follows it –, and the board's columns, the teams and the balls of a team stop at their memory-safety ceilings (`MEMORY_CEILINGS` in lib/uncap.ts: 1,000 columns, 4 teams – any count from 3 plays the quadrants –, 500 balls a team; the link keeps the typed value and the panel names the ceiling). A whirl or a blast reaching past the board takes what lies on it in work bounded by the board: `whirlReach()` walks (and the canvas draws) the arms only out to the board's diagonal, a blast's loop stops at the board's edges and its ring is drawn no wider than any canvas (`visualValue()`). With a duration search the finder says the run is the countdown (`Territory.finderFixed`) and points at the winner search.

### Maze Escape (oddplayground)
Feature odd-maze: `src/lib/physics/mazeGrid.ts` holds the pure maze – generation, the BFS distance map, the field geometry, the cell collision and the brains' choices – and `src/lib/physics/modes/maze.ts` the settings (URL keys `mzc`, `mzn`, `mzb`, `mzh`, `mzg`, `mzs`, `mzt`, `mztc`, `mzto`, `mzf`, `mzwc`, `mzd`, `mzbg`, `mzhud`, read and written by `readMazeParams()` / `writeMazeParams()`), the notes and the `MazeMode` plugin; `src/components/simulator/mazeRenderer.ts` (`MazeLayer`) draws it and `sections/MazeSection.tsx` is the "Maze" block of the Mode row. `tests/maze.test.ts` covers the maze (n − 1 passages, connected, deterministic), the distance map, the collision, the brains, the settings and full engine runs in the page's worlds (800 × 450 and 450 × 450, `lib/simulation/world.ts` – containment at every speed, pull, ball size and canvas, corridors a ball fits up to the columns' ceiling, determinism, the notes, explorers that always get out – also climbing at the slowest Speed under the strongest Pull –, the verdicts, the rig, a winner past the six teams, a resize, the finder).

- **The maze** – `generateMaze()` carves a perfect maze (a spanning tree) with an iterative recursive backtracker on the engine's seeded `random()` (the entrance column, the exit column, the start cell, then one draw per step with a choice), so a seed replays the same maze; `mazeRowsFor()` derives the rows from the portrait field (`MZ_FIELD_WIDTH` × `MZ_FIELD_HEIGHT` of the square the recorder crops to) and `buildMazeField()` fits the cells into it – a cell is 252 / columns world px in the world's 450 px square. The walls are `MZ_WALL_FRACTION` of a cell thick (at least `MZ_MIN_WALL` px) and a ball's radius is the Ball Size up to `MZ_BALL_FRACTION` of a cell (at least `MZ_MIN_RADIUS` px); in a tiny cell the pixel minimums give way (a wall at most `MZ_MAX_WALL_FRACTION` of a cell, the smallest radius at most `MZ_MIN_RADIUS_FRACTION` of one: `mazeBallRadius()`), so the contact distance stays a fixed share of the cell and a ball fits every corridor up to the 200-column ceiling (with a 1 px wall and a 0.5 px ball it outgrew half a cell from about 116 columns on and no ball left the chute). Nothing changes up to about 50 columns. The distance map (`mazeDistances()`, BFS from the exit cell) is computed once at init: the pitch of every note, the HUD's bars, the rig's shortest way and the clip limit's verdict read it.
- **Collision** – a ball whose centre is in a cell can only touch that cell's four edges and the round posts at its corners (a wall of a neighbouring cell reaches in only through its end cap, the post), so `resolveMazeCell()` looks at nothing else: one-sided edge tests (a ball whose centre reached or crossed a closed edge is put back inside at the contact distance) and radial posts, rebounds mirrored (restitution 1). `onBallStep()` undoes the engine's move and moves the ball again in micro-steps of at most `MZ_MICRO_STEP` of its contact distance (more than `MZ_MAX_MICRO_STEPS` and it is slowed instead), following it from cell to cell through open passages only, so nothing tunnels at any speed. The entrance chute (row −1) is open toward the entrance until the ball is fully in; the exit gap is open to every ball – but, with a forced winner, only to it until it is out. The balls fly through each other (`ballsPassThrough`) and feel no engine gravity (`gravityScale` 0): the mode's own pull is `MZ_GRAVITY_BASE` field heights per s² × Pull, the cruising speed `MZ_SPEED_BASE` field widths per s × Speed.
- **Brains** – the steering decides once per cell, when the ball crosses the cell's centre line across the direction it came in, and nudges it toward the chosen passage with a seeded scatter (`MZ_STEER_SCATTER`: it ricochets down the corridor – every bounce a note), with the speed to climb a cell against the pull (`climbSpeed()`: `MZ_CLIMB` cells' worth of height; both speed caps – `MZ_MAX_SPEED` × the cruising speed in `onBallStep()` and in the nudge – admit it, which matters at a low Speed under a strong Pull: at Speed 0.25 and Pull 1 the climb in a 12-column maze takes 181 px/s against a cap of 156, and a capped ball bounced at the foot of every upward corridor). `wallFollowChoice()` (left / right hand; `alternate` gives the balls different hands), `explorerChoice()` (the exit when in sight, else a seeded pick among the unvisited passages, else back along its path – a depth-first search over the ball's own explored set; a cell counts as explored once the ball reached its centre line, so a corridor it only bounced into is tried again, and when `explorerExhausted()` finds it back at the root of its search with nothing left – the rig's seal turned it away from the exit – it starts a fresh search there instead of shuttling between two cells) and, for the rig's forced winner, `shortestChoice()`. `bounce` never steers (a small seeded scatter at every bounce keeps it out of periodic orbits).
- **Sound and scores** – a counted hit (`MZ_HIT_MIN` of the cruising speed along the normal – also one against a wall of the cell the ball just moved into) is a plain `hit` event at `mazeNoteFrequency()` – a C-major pentatonic ladder from C4 at the entrance's distance up to A6 at the exit – rate-limited per ball and overall, so melodies, instruments, hit samples, the beat lock and the music bed apply as everywhere; it credits a bounce in the team stats. The first ball out gets the run's only escape (`ctx.creditEscape`: the teams banner, the finder's winner outcome and the mode's verdict agree), a `gap` event (the wall-break sound, the camera's shake) and the banner; reaching the exit cell first triggers the camera's slow motion. The run ends when every ball is out or at the clip limit (the ball nearest the exit then takes the escape – the rig's forced winner whatever the distances) and finishes `MZ_FINISH_HOLD_MS` later, with the banner in the clip. The seventh and eighth balls play for no team: one of them winning credits no team (the teams banner then stays away and the maze's own banner shows the win).
- **Drawing** – `MazeLayer` caches the walls (merged runs, a soft glow, the red chute and slot, the exit chevron) and the trail (paint strokes the mode records only when a ball paints a cell or passage it had not painted, stamped incrementally) in layers of their own, flares the walls where they were just hit, draws the fog as one pixel per cell scaled up smoothly, and the halos, the badge, the HUD and the banner (with a roster the teams banner takes over at the end: `teamsRenderer.ts` pads the roster with the maze palette and hides its scoreboard under the HUD; with the HUD off and the scoreboard in the top-left corner the badge moves to the top-right one). The badge and the HUD shrink into the bands beside the maze column (`MIN_OVERLAY_FONT` at the smallest) so they never cover the maze or its chute – `data-mz-clear` says so. `drawOverlay()` returns the bottom of the badge and the HUD where they reach into the captions' column, so the top captions start below them. The canvas mirrors the state as `data-mz-*`.
- **Wiring** – `BATTLE_WINNER_MODES` (rigged.ts) gets the maze (forced winner, the finder's winner outcome judged at the run's end), `SLOW_VIEW_MODES` (camera.ts) too; `effectiveBallCount()` returns `mzBalls` (at most six teams), the finder's `ModeSettings.maze` / `createEngineForSettings()`, `simulateOutcomeRun()` (one team per ball, a seed dropped once another ball has won) and `MAZE_FINDER_BATCH`. The obstacle editor and the multiplier pickups stay with the ring modes (the maze owns its playfield); the Ball Speed slider does not apply (the maze has its own speed). Bounce math: every counted wall hit is a bounce (`ctx.creditBounce`), the winner's break-out through the exit a break (`BOUNCE_MATH_BREAK_MODES`), and a pitch rule shifts the ball's notes (`shiftedObstacleFrequency()`). The desktop AI studio tunes the `mz*` settings (`MODE_PREFIXES`). Every number is uncapped (see Uncapped everything): a value typed past its slider runs as typed – a pull of 50, a speed of 1000, a ten-minute clip limit, a trail or fog past 1 (drawn fully opaque) –, and the columns and the balls stop at their memory-safety ceilings (`MEMORY_CEILINGS` in lib/uncap.ts: 200 columns – 286 rows, 57,200 cells –, 32 balls – the paint masks hold a bit per ball –; the link keeps the typed value and the panel names the ceiling). An absurd speed or pull melts instead of tunnelling: a ball's travel per frame stays within `MZ_MAX_MICRO_STEPS` micro-steps (it slows down), and a jump that is not one cell through an open passage is put back into its cell.

### Bounce math
Feature bounce-math: "add more bounciness on every bounce – customisable, mathematical – for every parameter, some on the beat of a song".

- **Rules and maths** – `src/lib/simulation/bounceMath.ts` (pure, no DOM; `tests/bounceMath.test.ts`): a `BounceRule` is `{ param, trigger, every, op, amount, formula?, min?, max?, scope }` over `BOUNCE_PARAMS` (bounciness, speed, size, gravity, rotation, gap, thickness, damping, trail, hue, pitch, timeScale, wobble, balls), `BOUNCE_TRIGGERS` (bounce, pass, collide, break, beat, bar, second, start) and `BOUNCE_OPS` (add, subtract, multiply, divide, power, root, modulo, set, random, formula). `applyRule(value, rule, ctx)` runs the operation, clamps to the rule's own min / max (none by default – no upper limit, the owner's rule), rounds counts and then fits the parameter's domain (`BOUNCE_PARAM_DOMAINS`): NaN, ±Infinity or a value below what the parameter can mean (a negative or zero size, a zero speed…) is rejected and the value stays as it was; no ceiling clamps it any more (`FLOAT_CEILING` is the largest float, and the thickness, damping, time-scale and wobble domains are open above): only the gap stays within its ring (`MAX_GAP`, a gap wider than the ring means nothing) and a trail keeps at most `MAX_TRAIL_POINTS` = `TRAIL_POINT_CEILING` (200) points (memory: every ball draws its own every frame). A ball whose numbers overflow the float range anyway is put back at the centre at the Ball Speed (`SPEED_CEILING` is the float range itself). Formulas (`compileFormula()`): a recursive-descent parser for + − * / ^ ( ) (^ right-associative and above unary minus), v n t b r, pi and e, sin cos tan abs sqrt pow min max floor ceil round log exp clamp; it compiles once into a postfix program (`Int32Array` of op / argument pairs and a `Float64Array` of constants) evaluated per fire on its own preallocated stack – never `eval` or `Function`, no allocation. Errors come back with a code, a position and the name (`FormulaError`), which the panel translates. `serializeRules()` / `parseRules()` are the URL form (`bmr`: `param.trigger.every.op.amount[.min][.max][.scope]`, rules joined by `;`, at most `MAX_BOUNCE_RULES` = `BOUNCE_RULE_CEILING` (1,000) rules – the list's memory-safety ceiling – and any every-N from 1 up (no 1,000,000 cap); numbers – every N too – write their decimal point as `_` because `.` separates the fields, a formula is percent-encoded in the amount field, `all` is the only scope written; the parse is tolerant – an invalid rule is dropped, the rest stays). `bm` stays the beat markers' key. `BOUNCE_MATH_PRESETS` are the six presets, `addRule` / `updateRule` / `moveRule` / `removeRule` / `appendPreset` the panel's list edits, `rotateHue()` the colour shift.
- **Engine** – `src/lib/physics/bounceMathRuntime.ts` (`BounceMathRuntime`, owned by `PhysicsEngine`, wired into `engine.ts` in delimited `--- bounce-math ---` blocks). The rules travel in the physics config (`PhysicsConfig.bounceMath` = rules, the beat grid, the canvas values' starting points, Show values: `bounceMathConfigOf()`), so the seed finder, split-screen arenas, the batch renderer and the fast export – which all build their engines from `engine.config` – replay a rule run exactly. The hooks only queue a trigger (preallocated, allocation-free): the ring bounce and the obstacle / editor-obstacle hits (`BM_BOUNCE`), the gap pass (`notePass()`, once per pass), the ball pair and a merge (`BM_COLLIDE`, both balls), a mode's own bounces (`ctx.creditBounce()` – String Battle – and the new optional `ctx.noteBounce()` that Bouncing Shapes, Glass Smash and the Journey's glass stage call), and the step's "gap" sound events for wall breaks (paired with the balls `reportWallBreak()` / `ctx.creditWallBreak()` reported). The start trigger fires at the start of a run's first step; at the end of every step the beats and bars of the grid (`beatSchedule.ts`: the grid Beat Drop and Picture Paint follow – the page's `rhythmBeat`, i.e. the loaded song's beats, an imported video's or the hand-placed markers, else the Sound section's BPM; a bar is every beat whose index is a multiple of four) and the whole seconds that fell into the step are queued, and the queue applies in order – each trigger's rules in list order, every N-th time. Random numbers come from the engine's seeded `random()` (only "random" rules and formulas that read r draw). A run without rules never enters any of this and replays exactly as before (`tests/extras.test.ts` still reproduces every mode's recorded trajectory).
  - Per ball: bounciness is `ball.restitution` (a factor on the engine's ring rebounds; on the obstacles' restitution – applied after their 0.98 cap (`rebound(…, ballRestitution, liftSpeed)` in obstacles.ts), so it has no upper limit: a 2 turns a 0.7 peg into a 1.4 rebound, and what it adds is bounded by a lift speed – the ball's cruising speed × the restitution scale × the bounciness, what a ring rebound sets – so a ball bouncing between pegs settles there instead of gaining speed hit after hit (Ball Drop, Bullseye, the obstacle editor, Bullseye's stuck darts, a knock from below on Glass Smash's and the Journey's panes); on Glass Smash's and the Journey's pane landings it scales the hop (a 2 hops twice as fast, four times as high); and – at most once, never compounding – on a rebound a mode sets itself – Bouncing Shapes' mirror walls report theirs through `ctx.noteBounce(ball, true)` and each shape's own speed carries the bounciness exactly once; the step planner's rebound bound includes it); speed and size go through the multipliers (`MultiplierRuntime.apply()`: the speed scale sets the velocity at once and the cruising speed from then on; the size refits the ball into its corridor, bursting rings it no longer fits, and a ball bigger than the arena ends the run with the multipliers' OUTGREW THE ARENA finish – in a mode without rings it stops at 45 % of the canvas' smaller side), so the multipliers' planner sub-steps fast balls (up to 64 sub-steps, then time dilation) and nothing tunnels; hue turns `ball.color` (`hueShift`; white, black and greys – the default ball is white – have no hue of their own, so `rotateHue()` turns them from a saturated colour of a similar lightness and the shift always shows), pitch is `ball.pitchShift` – the ring and obstacle hits of the ball (and Bouncing Shapes' wall notes) carry the shifted pitch in the sound event's `frequency` (the existing pitch override), nothing else in the audio changes. Clones and split halves inherit them.
  - World: gravity, the ring spin and the gap size go into the engine's config (the gap in place, like a keyframed gap, in the modes whose rings are built from it); `engine.config` hands out the page's values meanwhile and the next run starts from them again; a page patch that merely re-sends the page's value keeps the rule's, a new value replaces it. Air drag, the clock scale (`clockScale()`: each step covers that much more simulated time, planned like a multiplier run – in the modes the engine integrates itself), the trail length (the engine's trail ring buffer, up to 200 points) and the canvas' wall thickness and wobble (read by `components/simulator/bounceMathRenderer.ts`, so the fast export draws them too) are held by the runtime; balls spawns copies of the ball (the multipliers' clone with a seeded turn) within Multiply's memory-safe ceiling of 200 balls (with No limits on the ceiling is lifted like Multiply's: the copies fill the run's full-physics balls and the rest join the crowd).
  - A parameter a mode has no use for is ignored there (`bounceParamApplies()`: bounciness, speed and size in the ten ring modes, Ball Drop, Bouncing Shapes, Glass Smash, Bullseye and Journey; the gap in Classic, Accumulation and Multiply; balls in the ring modes; the clock where the engine integrates the balls) and the panel marks the rule.
  - Every mode reports its bounces: the engine's rings and obstacles, and every mode that resolves its own contacts calls `ctx.noteBounce(ball)` – String Battle, Bouncing Shapes, Glass Smash and the Journey's panes, the Collision Playground's container, the Multipliers board's walls, pegs and bars, the arena games' and the race's walls and obstacles, Beat Drop's landings, Paddle Keep-Up's walls, ceiling and paddle, Power Layers' stack hits, Circle Illusion's contacts, Bullseye's stuck darts – and in the rhythm modes without contacts the ball's note counts as its bounce (a Pendulum Wave bob's swing note, a harp pluck, a Polyrhythm dot's turn, a Sound Vortex ring, Beat Runner's landings). Ball-to-ball hits a mode resolves itself go through `ctx.noteCollide(a, b)` (the Collision Playground's orbs and lollipops, the Multipliers board, the arena games' clashes, the sparring double pendulums), next to the engine's own pair pass. `bounceTriggerApplies()` says where a trigger can never fire – a gap pass outside the ring modes and the Journey, a ball hit where balls pass through each other, a wall break where nothing breaks – and the rule row shows `BounceMath.triggerNotInMode` (`data-applies="0"`).
- **Page** – `components/simulator/sections/BounceMathSection.tsx` (search keys `BOUNCE_MATH_KEYS` in `SECTION_KEYS.ball`): the rule rows (parameter, trigger, every N, operation, amount with a comfort slider or the formula with live validation and an example line, min / max, this ball / all balls, ↑ ↓ ✕), + Add rule, the preset picker, Clear, Show values and the live readout (polled from `engine.getBounceMathView()` ten times a second). Simulator.tsx sends `bounceMathConfigOf(settings, rhythmBeat)` in an effect (new rules – or a new beat while a rule may follow it – drop a found seed like new keyframes), carries the rules over to another mode and hands the arenas the page's config. `bounceMathRenderer.ts` draws the Show values badge in the recorded square (bottom-left above the page's buttons, the bottom text and the song bar; top-right below the scoreboard and SLOW-MO while bottom captions show) and mirrors `data-bm-*` (rules, fires, total, the last ball's bounciness / colour / speed / size, gravity, balls, clock, the badge's rectangle). Words live in `Controls.bounceMath*` and the `BounceMath` namespace of `messages/*.json`.
- **Add a parameter**: add it to `BOUNCE_PARAMS`, `BOUNCE_PARAM_KIND`, `BOUNCE_PARAM_DOMAINS` and `BOUNCE_PARAM_SCALE`, read and write it in `BounceMathRuntime.applyBall()` / `applyWorld()` (and in `bounceParamApplies()` if some modes ignore it), and add `BounceMath.param.<name>` to every messages file. **Add a trigger**: add it to `BOUNCE_TRIGGERS` (its code is its index), call `note()` where the engine or a mode sees it (or queue it in `endStep()` for a clock-driven one) and add `BounceMath.trigger.<name>` / `everyUnit.<name>`.
